### Q1. MHT CET 2026 (20 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the density of fcc crystal structure having molar mass $58.5$ g mol$^{-1}$ and edge length $564$ pm ($N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23}$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.17$ g/cm$^3$
- **(B)** $1.89$ g/cm$^3$
- **(C)** $4.12$ g/cm$^3$
- **(D)** $3.22$ g/cm$^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $2.17$ g/cm$^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The formula for the density of a unit cell is given by $\rho = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times a^3}$

For an fcc crystal lattice, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

Given:
Molar mass $M = 58.5$ g mol$^{-1}$
Edge length $a = 564$ pm $= 5.64 \times 10^{-8}$ cm
Avogadro's number $N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23}$ mol$^{-1}$

Volume of the unit cell $a^3 = (5.64 \times 10^{-8})^3 \approx 1.794 \times 10^{-22}$ cm$^3$

Substituting the values into the formula:
$\rho = \dfrac{4 \times 58.5}{6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 1.794 \times 10^{-22}}$

$\rho = \dfrac{234}{108.03}$

$\rho \approx 2.166$ g/cm$^3$

Rounding off, we get $2.17$ g/cm$^3$.

Answer: $2.17$ g/cm$^3$

</details>

### Q2. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The unit cell of metal is fcc. Calculate the void volume unit cell if the radius of metal atom is $127.3$ pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.2142 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$
- **(B)** $3.4558 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$
- **(C)** $2.0221 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$
- **(D)** $2.4284 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.2142 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an fcc (face-centered cubic) unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

The total volume occupied by the atoms in the unit cell is:
$V_{\text{atoms}} = 4 \times \dfrac{4}{3} \pi r^3$

Given the radius of the metal atom $r = 127.3 \text{ pm} = 127.3 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm}$:
$V_{\text{atoms}} = \dfrac{16}{3} \times 3.1416 \times (127.3 \times 10^{-10})^3 \approx 3.4558 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

The packing efficiency of an fcc unit cell is $74\%$, meaning the atoms occupy $0.74$ of the total unit cell volume, and the void fraction is $1 - 0.74 = 0.26$.

The ratio of the void volume to the volume occupied by atoms is:
$\dfrac{V_{\text{void}}}{V_{\text{atoms}}} = \dfrac{0.26}{0.74}$

Therefore, the void volume is:
$V_{\text{void}} = 3.4558 \times 10^{-23} \times \dfrac{0.26}{0.74} = 1.2142 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Answer: $1.2142 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$

</details>

### Q3. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the radius of a particle present in fcc unit cell having edge length $200 \text{ pm}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $7.07 \times 10^{-9} \text{ cm}$
- **(B)** $3.535 \times 10^{-9} \text{ cm}$
- **(C)** $1.4140 \times 10^{-9} \text{ cm}$
- **(D)** $8.71 \times 10^{-9} \text{ cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $7.07 \times 10^{-9} \text{ cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the particles touch each other along the face diagonal.

The relationship between the radius of the particle $r$ and the edge length $a$ is given by:

$4r = \sqrt{2}a$

$r = \dfrac{\sqrt{2}a}{4} = \dfrac{a}{2\sqrt{2}}$

Given $a = 200 \text{ pm} = 200 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm} = 2 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$.

Substituting the value of $a$:

$r = \dfrac{2 \times 10^{-8}}{2\sqrt{2}} \text{ cm}$

$r = \dfrac{10^{-8}}{\sqrt{2}} \text{ cm}$

$r = \dfrac{\sqrt{2}}{2} \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

$r = 0.707 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm} = 7.07 \times 10^{-9} \text{ cm}$

Answer: $7.07 \times 10^{-9} \text{ cm}$

</details>

### Q4. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which of the following metals has simple cubic structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Potassium
- **(B)** Polonium
- **(C)** Calcium
- **(D)** Magnesium

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Polonium**

**Detailed Solution:**

Polonium is the only metal that crystallizes in a simple cubic structure.

Potassium crystallizes in a body-centered cubic (BCC) structure.

Calcium crystallizes in a face-centered cubic (FCC) structure.

Magnesium crystallizes in a hexagonal close-packed (HCP) structure.

Answer: Polonium

</details>

### Q5. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the void volume of a unit cell of polonium metal if the radius of polonium is $190$ pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.756 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$
- **(B)** $1.427 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$
- **(C)** $2.613 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$
- **(D)** $5.487 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2.613 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Polonium crystallizes in a simple cubic lattice.

For a simple cubic unit cell, the edge length $a$ is related to the atomic radius $r$ by $a = 2r$.

Given $r = 190 \text{ pm} = 1.9 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$.

$a = 2 \times 1.9 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm} = 3.8 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Volume of the unit cell, $V_{\text{cell}} = a^3 = (3.8 \times 10^{-8})^3 = 54.872 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3 = 5.487 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

The number of atoms per unit cell in a simple cubic lattice is $Z = 1$.

Volume occupied by the atom, $V_{\text{atom}} = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3$

$V_{\text{atom}} = \dfrac{4}{3} \times \dfrac{22}{7} \times (1.9 \times 10^{-8})^3 = 28.74 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3 = 2.874 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Void volume $= V_{\text{cell}} - V_{\text{atom}}$

Void volume $= 5.487 \times 10^{-23} - 2.874 \times 10^{-23} = 2.613 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Answer: $2.613 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$

</details>

### Q6. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the molar mass of a metal having density $10$ $\text{g cm}^{-3}$ and fcc structure with edge length $400$ pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $104.5\,\text{g mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $96.35\,\text{g mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $146.0\,\text{g mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $79.4\,\text{g mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $96.35\,\text{g mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times a^3}$

Rearranging for molar mass $M$:

$M = \dfrac{d \times N_A \times a^3}{Z}$

For an fcc (face-centered cubic) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

Given:
$d = 10 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$
$a = 400 \text{ pm} = 400 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm} = 4 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$
$N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23} \text{ mol}^{-1}$

Calculating the volume of the unit cell $a^3$:

$a^3 = (4 \times 10^{-8})^3 = 64 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

Substituting the values into the formula:

$M = \dfrac{10 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 64 \times 10^{-24}}{4}$

$M = \dfrac{10 \times 6.022 \times 10^{-1} \times 64}{4}$

$M = 6.022 \times 16$

$M = 96.352 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $96.35\,\text{g mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q7. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the total number of atoms present per fcc unit cell on all its faces only ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$ atom
- **(B)** $1$ atom
- **(C)** $4$ atom
- **(D)** $3$ atom

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3$ atom**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, atoms are present at the $8$ corners and at the centers of all $6$ faces.

The atoms present at the face centers are shared equally between two adjacent unit cells. Thus, the contribution of each face-centered atom to one unit cell is $\dfrac{1}{2}$.

Number of faces in a cubic unit cell = $6$

Total number of atoms present on all faces per unit cell = $6 \times \dfrac{1}{2} = 3$ atoms.

Answer: $3$ atom

</details>

### Q8. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the mass of 5 unit cells of sodium element having bcc structure
(molar mass of $\text{Na} = 23$ g/mol)?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.05 \times 10^{-22}\ \text{g}$
- **(B)** $4.123 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{g}$
- **(C)** $7.638 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{g}$
- **(D)** $3.819 \times 10^{-22}\ \text{g}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3.819 \times 10^{-22}\ \text{g}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of atoms in one bcc unit cell of sodium, $Z = 2$

Number of atoms in $5$ unit cells $= 5 \times 2 = 10$

Mass of one sodium atom $= \dfrac{M}{N_A} = \dfrac{23}{6.022 \times 10^{23}}\ \text{g}$

Mass of $5$ unit cells $= 10 \times \dfrac{23}{6.022 \times 10^{23}}\ \text{g}$

$= \dfrac{230}{6.022 \times 10^{23}}\ \text{g}$

$= 38.193 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{g}$

$= 3.819 \times 10^{-22}\ \text{g}$

Answer: $3.819 \times 10^{-22}\ \text{g}$

</details>

### Q9. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a solid, oxide ions are arranged in ccp. Cation A occupies $1/8$th of the tetrahedral voids and cation B occupies $1/4$th of the octahedral voids. What is the formula of the compound?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\text{ABO}_4$
- **(B)** $\text{AB}_2\text{O}_3$
- **(C)** $\text{A}_2\text{BO}_4$
- **(D)** $\text{AB}_2\text{O}_4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\text{ABO}_4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the number of oxide ions in the ccp lattice be $N$.

The number of tetrahedral voids generated is $2N$ and the number of octahedral voids generated is $N$.

Number of cations A occupying tetrahedral voids = $\dfrac{1}{8} \times 2N = \dfrac{N}{4}$

Number of cations B occupying octahedral voids = $\dfrac{1}{4} \times N = \dfrac{N}{4}$

The ratio of atoms A, B, and O is $\dfrac{N}{4} : \dfrac{N}{4} : N = 1 : 1 : 4$

The formula of the compound is $\text{ABO}_4$.

Answer: $\text{ABO}_4$

</details>

---

### Q10. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the relationship between the radius of a particle ($r$) and edge length ($a$) for bcc unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $r = a/2$
- **(B)** $r = \sqrt{3}a/4$
- **(C)** $r = \sqrt{2}a/4$
- **(D)** $r = a/\sqrt{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $r = \sqrt{3}a/4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, the particles touch each other along the body diagonal.

The length of the body diagonal of a cube with edge length $a$ is $\sqrt{3}a$.

Since the particles at the corners touch the particle at the body center, the body diagonal is equal to $4r$, where $r$ is the radius of the particle.

$4r = \sqrt{3}a$

$r = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}a}{4}$

Answer: $r = \sqrt{3}a/4$

</details>

### Q11. MHT CET 2026 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The compound is formed of two different elements A and B. Atoms of B forms hcp structure and atoms of A occupy 2/3rd of octahedral voids. Calculate the formula of compound.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\text{AB}$
- **(B)** $\text{AB}_2$
- **(C)** $\text{A}_2\text{B}$
- **(D)** $\text{A}_2\text{B}_3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\text{A}_2\text{B}_3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the number of atoms of element B in the hcp structure be $N$.

In a close-packed structure, the number of octahedral voids generated is equal to the number of atoms in the packing. Thus, the number of octahedral voids is $N$.

Atoms of element A occupy $\dfrac{2}{3}$ of the octahedral voids.

Number of atoms of A $= \dfrac{2}{3} \times N$

The ratio of the number of atoms of A to that of B is:

$\text{A} : \text{B} = \dfrac{2}{3}N : N = 2 : 3$

The formula of the compound is $\text{A}_2\text{B}_3$.

Answer: $\text{A}_2\text{B}_3$

</details>

---

### Q12. MHT CET 2026 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find the type of unit cell if the density is $6.25$ g/cm$^3$, edge length $400$ pm and the atomic mass of an element is $120$ g/mol.
(Consider $N_A = 6.02 \times 10^{23}$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** Base centred
- **(B)** bcc
- **(C)** fcc
- **(D)** sc

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) bcc**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times a^3}$

Rearranging the formula to solve for the number of atoms per unit cell $Z$:

$Z = \dfrac{d \times N_A \times a^3}{M}$

Given values are:
$d = 6.25 \text{ g/cm}^3$
$a = 400 \text{ pm} = 4 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$
$M = 120 \text{ g/mol}$
$N_A = 6.02 \times 10^{23} \text{ mol}^{-1}$

Calculating the volume of the unit cell $a^3$:

$a^3 = (4 \times 10^{-8})^3 = 64 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

Substituting the values into the expression for $Z$:

$Z = \dfrac{6.25 \times 6.02 \times 10^{23} \times 64 \times 10^{-24}}{120}$

$Z = \dfrac{400 \times 6.02 \times 10^{-1}}{120}$

$Z = \dfrac{240.8}{120} \approx 2$

Since the number of atoms per unit cell is $2$, the unit cell is body-centered cubic (bcc).

Answer: bcc

</details>

### Q13. MHT CET 2026 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume occupied by particles of a fcc unit cell if the radius of the atom present in it is $100$ pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $9.931 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$
- **(B)** $7.452 \times 10^{-25}\,\text{cm}^3$
- **(C)** $2.615 \times 10^{-25}\,\text{cm}^3$
- **(D)** $1.677 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.677 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Radius of the atom, $r = 100 \text{ pm} = 100 \times 10^{-12} \text{ m} = 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

In a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the effective number of atoms is $Z = 4$.

Volume occupied by the particles (atoms) in the unit cell is given by:

$V = Z \times \dfrac{4}{3} \pi r^3$

Substituting the values:

$V = 4 \times \dfrac{4}{3} \times \dfrac{22}{7} \times (10^{-8})^3$

$V = \dfrac{352}{21} \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

$V \approx 16.76 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

$V \approx 1.677 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Answer: $1.677 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q14. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Find out the number of unit cells present in $100$ g of an element if density of it is $9.0$ $\text{g cm}^{-3}$.
[Volume of unit cell $= 4.445 \times 10^{-23}\text{cm}^3$]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.0 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $3.5 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $2.5 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $2.0 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2.5 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Total volume of the element $V = \dfrac{\text{Mass}}{\text{Density}}$

$V = \dfrac{100}{9.0} \text{ cm}^3$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{\text{Total volume}}{\text{Volume of one unit cell}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{100}{9.0 \times 4.445 \times 10^{-23}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{100}{40.005 \times 10^{-23}}$

Number of unit cells $\approx 2.5 \times 10^{23}$

Answer: $2.5 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

### Q15. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A metal crystallizes to form fcc unit cells. Calculate the void volume of a unit cell if the edge length of the unit cell is $390$ pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.847 \times 10^{-23}$ $\text{cm}^3$
- **(B)** $1.898 \times 10^{-23}$ $\text{cm}^3$
- **(C)** $1.542 \times 10^{-23}$ $\text{cm}^3$
- **(D)** $5.932 \times 10^{-23}$ $\text{cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.542 \times 10^{-23}$ $\text{cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The edge length of the unit cell is $a = 390 \text{ pm} = 3.9 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$.

The total volume of the unit cell is $V = a^3 = (3.9 \times 10^{-8})^3 \text{ cm}^3 = 59.319 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3 = 5.932 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$.

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the packing efficiency is $74\%$.

The percentage of void space in the fcc unit cell is $100\% - 74\% = 26\%$.

The void volume of the unit cell is $0.26 \times V$.

Substituting the value of $V$, we get $0.26 \times 5.932 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3 = 1.542 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$.

Answer: $1.542 \times 10^{-23}$ $\text{cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q16. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Copper crystallises in a face-centred cubic lattice with a unit cell length of $361$ pm. What is the radius of copper atom in pm?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $157.6$
- **(B)** $181.5$
- **(C)** $108.4$
- **(D)** $127.6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $127.6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centred cubic (fcc) lattice, the atoms touch each other along the face diagonal.

The length of the face diagonal is $\sqrt{2}a$, where $a$ is the edge length of the unit cell.

Therefore, $4r = \sqrt{2}a$

$r = \dfrac{\sqrt{2}a}{4} = \dfrac{a}{2\sqrt{2}}$

Given $a = 361$ pm.

$r = \dfrac{361}{2\sqrt{2}} = \dfrac{361}{2 \times 1.414} = 127.6$ pm.

Answer: $127.6$

</details>

### Q17. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What type of unit cell is formed by silver?

**Options:**

- **(A)** BCC
- **(B)** FCC
- **(C)** SC
- **(D)** Base centered

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) FCC**

**Detailed Solution:**

Silver (Ag) crystallizes in a face-centered cubic (FCC) or cubic close-packed (ccp) lattice structure.

In this arrangement, silver atoms are located at all the corners and at the centers of all the faces of the cubic unit cell.

Therefore, silver forms an FCC unit cell.

Answer: FCC

### Q18. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms in $0.6$ g metal that forms bcc structure having density $8.5$ g cm$^{-3}$, if the volume of unit cell is $3.6 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.8 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $3.92 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $4.7 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $4.15 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3.92 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Volume of the metal $V = \dfrac{\text{Mass}}{\text{Density}} = \dfrac{0.6}{8.5}$ cm$^3$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{V}{\text{Volume of one unit cell}} = \dfrac{0.6}{8.5 \times 3.6 \times 10^{-23}}$

Since the metal forms a bcc structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Total number of atoms $= 2 \times \dfrac{0.6}{8.5 \times 3.6 \times 10^{-23}}$

$= \dfrac{1.2}{30.6 \times 10^{-23}}$

$= 0.0392 \times 10^{23} = 3.92 \times 10^{21}$

Answer: $3.92 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q19. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A compound is formed by two elements X and Y, atoms of the element Y as anion make ccp and those of the element X as cation occupy all the octahedral voids. What is the formula of the compound ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\text{X}_3\text{Y}_4$
- **(B)** $\text{X}_4\text{Y}_3$
- **(C)** $\text{X}_2\text{Y}_3$
- **(D)** $\text{XY}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\text{XY}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a cubic close-packed (ccp) lattice, let the number of atoms forming the lattice be $N$.

The number of octahedral voids generated in a ccp lattice is equal to the number of atoms forming the lattice, which is $N$.

Since atoms of element Y form the ccp lattice, the number of Y atoms is $N$.

Since atoms of element X occupy all the octahedral voids, the number of X atoms is also $N$.

The ratio of the number of atoms of X to that of Y is $N : N = 1 : 1$.

Therefore, the formula of the compound is $\text{XY}$.

Answer: $\text{XY}$

</details>

### Q20. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the volume of fcc unit cell having radius of particle $180$ pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.32 \times 10^{-22}\,cm^3$
- **(B)** $4.67 \times 10^{-22}\,cm^3$
- **(C)** $7.20 \times 10^{-22}\,cm^3$
- **(D)** $2.46 \times 10^{-22}\,cm^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.32 \times 10^{-22}\,cm^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an fcc unit cell, the relationship between edge length $a$ and radius $r$ is given by:

$a = 2\sqrt{2}r$

Given $r = 180$ pm $= 180 \times 10^{-10}$ cm.

$a = 2\sqrt{2} \times 180 \times 10^{-10}$ cm $= 360\sqrt{2} \times 10^{-10}$ cm

Volume of the unit cell $V = a^3$

$V = (360\sqrt{2} \times 10^{-10})^3$ cm$^3$

$V = 46656000 \times 2\sqrt{2} \times 10^{-30}$ cm$^3$

$V = 131964271 \times 10^{-30}$ cm$^3 \approx 1.32 \times 10^{-22}$ cm$^3$

Answer: $1.32 \times 10^{-22}\,cm^3$

</details>

### Q21. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Sodium crystallizes in the bcc structure. If the edge length of a unit cell is $4.3 \times 10^{-8}$ cm, calculate the radius of an atom.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.86 \times 10^{-8}$ cm
- **(B)** $1.52 \times 10^{-8}$ cm
- **(C)** $2.15 \times 10^{-8}$ cm
- **(D)** $4.3 \times 10^{-8}$ cm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.86 \times 10^{-8}$ cm**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure, the atoms touch each other along the body diagonal of the unit cell.

The relationship between the radius of the atom $r$ and the edge length of the unit cell $a$ is given by:

$4r = \sqrt{3}a$

Given $a = 4.3 \times 10^{-8}$ cm.

$r = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}}{4} \times 4.3 \times 10^{-8}$ cm

$r = \dfrac{1.732}{4} \times 4.3 \times 10^{-8}$ cm

$r = 0.433 \times 4.3 \times 10^{-8}$ cm

$r = 1.86 \times 10^{-8}$ cm

Answer: $1.86 \times 10^{-8}$ cm

</details>

---

### Q22. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A compound is formed by two elements A and B. The elements B forms cubic close packed structure and atoms of A occupy $1/3$rd of tetrahedral voids. If the formula of the compound is $A_xB_y$, then find the value of $(x+y)$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4$
- **(B)** $3$
- **(C)** $2$
- **(D)** $5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $5$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the number of atoms of element B forming the cubic close packed (ccp) lattice be $N$.

The number of tetrahedral voids generated in a ccp lattice is $2N$.

Since atoms of element A occupy $1/3$rd of the tetrahedral voids, the number of atoms of A is $\dfrac{1}{3} \times 2N = \dfrac{2N}{3}$.

The ratio of the number of atoms of A to that of B is $\dfrac{2N}{3} : N = 2 : 3$.

Thus, the simplest formula of the compound is $A_2B_3$.

Comparing this with the given formula $A_xB_y$, we get $x = 2$ and $y = 3$.

Therefore, the value of $(x + y)$ is $2 + 3 = 5$.

Answer: $5$

</details>

---

### Q23. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the maximum number of nearest neighbors for a given lattice point in the hcp structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4$
- **(B)** $6$
- **(C)** $8$
- **(D)** $12$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $12$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a hexagonal close-packed (hcp) structure, the atoms are arranged in an ABAB... pattern.

For any given atom in a particular layer, there are $6$ nearest neighbors in the same plane.

Additionally, there are $3$ nearest neighbors in the layer immediately above it and $3$ nearest neighbors in the layer immediately below it.

Total number of nearest neighbors = $6 + 3 + 3 = 12$.

Thus, the coordination number or the maximum number of nearest neighbors for a given lattice point in the hcp structure is $12$.

Answer: $12$

</details>

---

### Q24. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A metal crystallizes in a bcc unit cell having unit cell volume $2.5 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$. Find the number of unit cells in 18 g metal if the density of metal is $7.2\ \text{g cm}^{-3}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.0 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $2.0 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $0.5 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $1.5 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.0 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Total volume of the metal is given by the ratio of mass to density.

$V_{\text{total}} = \dfrac{\text{Mass}}{\text{Density}} = \dfrac{18}{7.2} = 2.5\ \text{cm}^3$

The number of unit cells is the total volume divided by the volume of one unit cell.

$\text{Number of unit cells} = \dfrac{V_{\text{total}}}{V_{\text{unit cell}}} = \dfrac{2.5}{2.5 \times 10^{-23}} = 1.0 \times 10^{23}$

Answer: $1.0 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

---

### Q25. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A metal has a fcc structure if the edge length of unit cell is 200 pm, calculate the volume occupied by particles in a unit cell.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.08 \times 10^{-24}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(B)** $5.92 \times 10^{-24}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(C)** $8 \times 10^{-24}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(D)** $3.14 \times 10^{-24}\ \text{cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $5.92 \times 10^{-24}\ \text{cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Edge length of the unit cell, $a = 200 \text{ pm} = 2 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Volume of the unit cell, $V = a^3 = (2 \times 10^{-8})^3 = 8 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) structure, the packing efficiency is $74\%$.

Volume occupied by particles in the unit cell = $0.74 \times V$

$= 0.74 \times 8 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

$= 5.92 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

Answer: $5.92 \times 10^{-24}\ \text{cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q26. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the radius of atom of metal forming fcc unit cell having edge length $360$ pm

**Options:**

- **(A)** $180.04$ pm
- **(B)** $127.26$ pm
- **(C)** $155.91$ pm
- **(D)** $254.67$ pm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $127.26$ pm**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the atoms touch each other along the face diagonal.

The relationship between the edge length $a$ and the atomic radius $r$ is given by:

$\sqrt{2}a = 4r$

$r = \dfrac{a}{2\sqrt{2}}$

Given $a = 360$ pm:

$r = \dfrac{360}{2\sqrt{2}} = 90\sqrt{2}$

Using $\sqrt{2} = 1.414$:

$r = 90 \times 1.414 = 127.26$ pm

Answer: $127.26$ pm

</details>

---

### Q27. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the coordination number of particle in simple cubic structure ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$
- **(B)** $4$
- **(C)** $6$
- **(D)** $12$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a simple cubic structure, each particle is located at the corners of the cubic unit cell.

The nearest neighbours of any particle are the particles situated at the adjacent corners along the edges of the unit cells.

For any given particle, there are $2$ nearest neighbours along the x-axis, $2$ along the y-axis, and $2$ along the z-axis.

Total number of nearest neighbours $= 2 + 2 + 2 = 6$.

Therefore, the coordination number of a particle in a simple cubic structure is $6$.

Answer: $6$

</details>

---

### Q28. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the density of an element having molar mass $225\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$ forming bcc structure $[a^3 \times N_A = 75\ \text{cm}^3\text{mol}^{-1}]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.0$ g cm$^{-3}$
- **(B)** $2.81$ g cm$^{-3}$
- **(C)** $9.24$ g cm$^{-3}$
- **(D)** $11.36$ g cm$^{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $6.0$ g cm$^{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

The formula for the density of a unit cell is given by:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

Substituting the given values $Z = 2$, $M = 225\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$, and $a^3 \times N_A = 75\ \text{cm}^3\text{mol}^{-1}$:

$d = \dfrac{2 \times 225}{75}$

$d = \dfrac{450}{75} = 6.0\ \text{g cm}^{-3}$

Answer: $6.0$ g cm$^{-3}$

</details>

---

### Q29. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the total volume of simple cubic unit cell in $\text{cm}^3$ if void volume is $2.0 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.2 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(B)** $3.8 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(C)** $3.4 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(D)** $4.6 \times 10^{-23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $4.2 \times 10^{-23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a simple cubic unit cell, the relation between edge length $a$ and radius of the atom $r$ is $a = 2r$.

The packing fraction is given by $\dfrac{\text{Volume of atoms}}{\text{Total volume}} = \dfrac{\dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3}{a^3} = \dfrac{\dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3}{8r^3} = \dfrac{\pi}{6} \approx 0.524$.

The fraction of void volume in the unit cell is $1 - 0.524 = 0.476$.

Given the void volume is $2.0 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$.

Total volume of the unit cell $V = \dfrac{\text{Void volume}}{\text{Fraction of void volume}} = \dfrac{2.0 \times 10^{-23}}{0.476}$.

$V \approx 4.2 \times 10^{-23}\,\text{cm}^3$.

Answer: $4.2 \times 10^{-23}$

</details>

---

### Q30. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In ionic solid, anions are arranged in hcp array and cations occupy $\dfrac{1}{2}$ tetrahedral voids. What is the formula of ionic compound ?
[Consider A = Cation ; B = anion]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\text{AB}_2$
- **(B)** $\text{AB}$
- **(C)** $\text{A}_2\text{B}$
- **(D)** $\text{AB}_3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\text{AB}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the number of anions (B) forming the hcp array be $N$.

The total number of tetrahedral voids generated in the lattice is $2N$.

Since cations (A) occupy $\dfrac{1}{2}$ of the tetrahedral voids, the number of cations (A) is $\dfrac{1}{2} \times 2N = N$.

The ratio of the number of cations (A) to the number of anions (B) is $N : N = 1 : 1$.

Therefore, the formula of the ionic compound is $\text{AB}$.

Answer: $\text{AB}$

</details>

### Q31. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Gold crystallizes as fcc unit cell, the edge length of unit cell is $408$ pm. What is the radius of gold atom?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $86.6$ pm
- **(B)** $115.4$ pm
- **(C)** $144.2$ pm
- **(D)** $175.2$ pm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $144.2$ pm**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the relationship between the edge length $a$ and the radius of the atom $r$ is given by:

$4r = \sqrt{2}a$

$r = \dfrac{a}{2\sqrt{2}}$

Given $a = 408$ pm:

$r = \dfrac{408}{2\sqrt{2}} = \dfrac{204}{\sqrt{2}} = 102\sqrt{2}$ pm

Substituting $\sqrt{2} = 1.414$:

$r = 102 \times 1.414 = 144.228$ pm $\approx 144.2$ pm

Answer: $144.2$ pm

</details>

### Q32. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the number of tetrahedral voids present in $0.5$ mole of compound forming hcp structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.223 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $3.628 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $4.487 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $6.022 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $6.022 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of atoms in $0.5$ mole of the compound $= 0.5 \times N_A = 0.5 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} = 3.011 \times 10^{23}$.

In a close-packed structure, the number of tetrahedral voids is twice the number of atoms forming the lattice.

Number of tetrahedral voids $= 2 \times 3.011 \times 10^{23} = 6.022 \times 10^{23}$.

Answer: $6.022 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

### Q33. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of unit cell in $0.5$ g metal if the product of volume and density of unit cell is $6.65 \times 10^{-23}$ g

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.340 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $7.518 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $5.625 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $8.492 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $7.518 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of one unit cell is given by the product of its volume and density.

Mass of one unit cell = $6.65 \times 10^{-23}$ g

Total mass of the metal = $0.5$ g

Number of unit cells = $\dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}$

Number of unit cells = $\dfrac{0.5}{6.65 \times 10^{-23}}$

Number of unit cells = $7.518 \times 10^{21}$

Answer: $7.518 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q34. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the total number of adjacent unit cells shared by each face particle of fcc unit cell ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1$
- **(B)** $2$
- **(C)** $4$
- **(D)** $8$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, particles are located at the corners and at the center of each face.

A particle present at the center of a face lies exactly on the boundary between two adjacent unit cells.

Therefore, each face-centered particle is shared equally by exactly $2$ unit cells.

Answer: $2$

</details>

### Q35. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A metal forms fcc structure. Calculate the volume of fcc unit cell in $\text{cm}^3$ if void volume is $1.66 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.912 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(B)** $8.151 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(C)** $9.346 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(D)** $6.385 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $6.385 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an fcc (face-centered cubic) structure, the packing efficiency is $74\%$.

The percentage of void volume in the fcc unit cell is $100\% - 74\% = 26\%$.

Let the total volume of the unit cell be $V$.

Void volume $= 0.26 V$

$0.26 V = 1.66 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$

$V = \dfrac{1.66 \times 10^{-23}}{0.26}\ \text{cm}^3$

$V = 6.3846 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3 \approx 6.385 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$

Answer: $6.385 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q36. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms present in $1\ \text{g}$ metal that forms simple unit cell structure if product of density and volume of unit cell is $66.5 \times 10^{-24}\ \text{g}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.504 \times 10^{22}$
- **(B)** $3.419 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $2.516 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $2.018 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.504 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a simple cubic unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 1$.

The mass of one unit cell is equal to the product of its density and volume.

Mass of one unit cell = $\rho \times V = 66.5 \times 10^{-24} \text{ g}$.

Since there is $1$ atom per unit cell, the mass of one atom is $66.5 \times 10^{-24} \text{ g}$.

The number of atoms in $1 \text{ g}$ of the metal is given by:

Number of atoms = $\dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one atom}}$

Number of atoms = $\dfrac{1}{66.5 \times 10^{-24}}$

Number of atoms = $0.015037 \times 10^{24}$

Number of atoms $\approx 1.504 \times 10^{22}$

</details>

### Q38. MHT CET 2026 (17 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms in 1 g metal that forms bcc crystal structure $[\rho \times a^3 = 6 \cdot 6 \times 10^{-22} \text{ g}]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 \cdot 05 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $5 \cdot 12 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $3 \cdot 03 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $2 \cdot 47 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3 \cdot 03 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The mass of one unit cell is given by the product of its density and volume:

Mass of unit cell $= \rho \times a^3 = 6 \cdot 6 \times 10^{-22} \text{ g}$

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) crystal structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Mass of one atom $= \dfrac{\text{Mass of unit cell}}{Z} = \dfrac{6 \cdot 6 \times 10^{-22}}{2} = 3 \cdot 3 \times 10^{-22} \text{ g}$

The number of atoms in $1 \text{ g}$ of the metal is:

Number of atoms $= \dfrac{1 \text{ g}}{\text{Mass of one atom}} = \dfrac{1}{3 \cdot 3 \times 10^{-22}} = 3 \cdot 03 \times 10^{21}$

Answer: $3 \cdot 03 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q40. MHT CET 2026 (17 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find the volume occupied by a particle in a simple cubic unit cell if the radius of a particle in it is 190 pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.410 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(B)** $3.625 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(C)** $5.715 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$
- **(D)** $2.873 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2.873 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The volume occupied by a particle is the volume of the spherical particle itself.

Given the radius of the particle, $r = 190 \text{ pm} = 190 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm} = 1.9 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$.

Volume of one particle is given by:

$V = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3$

Substituting the value of $r$:

$V = \dfrac{4}{3} \times 3.1416 \times (1.9 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm})^3$

$V = 4.1888 \times 6.859 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

$V = 28.73 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3 = 2.873 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Answer: $2.873 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$

</details>

### Q41. MHT CET 2026 (17 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of unit cells in $1\text{cm}^3$ of metal if it forms simple cubic structure with unit cell edge length 500 pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $2.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $8.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $3.0 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $8.0 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Edge length of the unit cell, $a = 500 \text{ pm} = 500 \times 10^{-12} \text{ m} = 5 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Volume of one unit cell, $V = a^3 = (5 \times 10^{-8})^3 \text{ cm}^3 = 125 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

Number of unit cells in $1 \text{ cm}^3 = \dfrac{\text{Total volume}}{\text{Volume of one unit cell}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{1}{125 \times 10^{-24}} = \dfrac{1000 \times 10^{21}}{125} = 8.0 \times 10^{21}$

Answer: $8.0 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q42. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the total contribution of all corner particles in bcc unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1 particle
- **(B)** 2 particles
- **(C)** 3 particles
- **(D)** 4 particles

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 1 particle**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, particles are present at all $8$ corners and $1$ particle is present at the body center.

Each corner particle is shared by $8$ adjacent unit cells. Thus, the contribution of one corner particle to a single unit cell is $\dfrac{1}{8}$.

Since there are $8$ corners in a unit cell, the total contribution of all corner particles is $8 \times \dfrac{1}{8} = 1$ particle.

Answer: 1 particle

</details>

### Q43. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

When silver crystallizes, it forms face centered cubic cells, if the volume of unit cells $6.84 \times 10^{-23}\ \text{cm}^3$. Calculate the density of silver. (Molar mass of silver is 108 g/mol, $N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23}$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $12.49\ \text{gm cm}^{-3}$
- **(B)** $10.49\ \text{gm cm}^{-3}$
- **(C)** $16.89\ \text{gm cm}^{-3}$
- **(D)** $20.49\ \text{gm cm}^{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $10.49\ \text{gm cm}^{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

The density of the unit cell is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times V}$

Substituting the given values:

$d = \dfrac{4 \times 108}{6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 6.84 \times 10^{-23}}$

$d = \dfrac{432}{41.19}$

$d = 10.49\ \text{gm cm}^{-3}$

Answer: $10.49\ \text{gm cm}^{-3}$

</details>

### Q44. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the coordination number of Ag in a crystal lattice?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4$
- **(B)** $6$
- **(C)** $12$
- **(D)** $8$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $12$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Silver (Ag) crystallizes in a face-centered cubic (fcc) or cubic close-packed (ccp) lattice structure.

In an fcc or ccp lattice, each atom is surrounded by $12$ nearest neighboring atoms ($6$ in its own layer, $3$ in the layer above, and $3$ in the layer below).

Therefore, the coordination number of Ag in its crystal lattice is $12$.

Answer: $12$

</details>

---

### Q45. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A compound forms hcp structure. What is the number of tetrahedral voids formed in 0.8 mol of it.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.818 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $9.635 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $7.227 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $2.410 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $9.635 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of atoms in $0.8$ mol of the compound is given by $0.8 \times N_A$.

Number of atoms $= 0.8 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} = 4.8176 \times 10^{23}$

In a close-packed structure, the number of tetrahedral voids is twice the number of atoms.

Number of tetrahedral voids $= 2 \times 4.8176 \times 10^{23} = 9.6352 \times 10^{23}$

Answer: $9.635 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

### Q46. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms present in 90 g of metal if it forms bcc structure.
[ $\rho \times a^3 = 1.8 \times 10^{-22}$ g ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.0 \times 10^{24}$
- **(B)** $1.0 \times 10^{24}$
- **(C)** $2.0 \times 10^{24}$
- **(D)** $3.0 \times 10^{24}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.0 \times 10^{24}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of one unit cell is given by $\rho \times a^3 = 1.8 \times 10^{-22}$ g.

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Mass of $2$ atoms $= 1.8 \times 10^{-22}$ g.

Mass of $1$ atom $= \dfrac{1.8 \times 10^{-22}}{2} = 0.9 \times 10^{-22}$ g.

Number of atoms in $90$ g of the metal $= \dfrac{90}{0.9 \times 10^{-22}} = 100 \times 10^{22} = 1.0 \times 10^{24}$.

Answer: $1.0 \times 10^{24}$

</details>

---

### Q47. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In the fcc structure, the number of tetrahedral holes per sphere is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1$
- **(B)** $4$
- **(C)** $2$
- **(D)** $8$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the total number of atoms (spheres) is $4$.

The tetrahedral voids are located on the body diagonals of the cube. There are $4$ body diagonals and each body diagonal contains $2$ tetrahedral voids.

Total number of tetrahedral voids in an fcc unit cell $= 4 \times 2 = 8$.

Number of tetrahedral holes per sphere $= \dfrac{8}{4} = 2$.

Answer: $2$

</details>

---

### Q48. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms present in 1.625 g metal if it forms bcc unit cell structure.
$[ \rho \times a^3 = 3.25 \times 10^{-22}\ \text{g}]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.0 \times 10^{22}$
- **(B)** $1.5 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $2.5 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $1.0 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.0 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of one unit cell is given by $\rho \times a^3 = 3.25 \times 10^{-22}$ g.

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Mass of one atom = $\dfrac{\text{Mass of unit cell}}{Z} = \dfrac{3.25 \times 10^{-22}}{2} = 1.625 \times 10^{-22}$ g.

Total number of atoms in $1.625$ g of the metal = $\dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one atom}}$

Number of atoms = $\dfrac{1.625}{1.625 \times 10^{-22}} = 1.0 \times 10^{22}$

Answer: $1.0 \times 10^{22}$

</details>

### Q49. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An element crystallises in fcc unit cell with cell edge length of $3.608 \times 10^{-8}$ cm, the density of element is $8.92 \text{ gcm}^{-3}$. Calculate the atomic mass of element ($\text{N}_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23}$).

**Options:**

- **(A)** $60$ g/mol
- **(B)** $65$ g/mol
- **(C)** $63$ g/mol
- **(D)** $108$ g/mol

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $63$ g/mol**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an fcc unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

The formula for the density of a unit cell is given by:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times a^3}$

Rearranging the formula to solve for the atomic mass $M$:

$M = \dfrac{d \times N_A \times a^3}{Z}$

Given values:
$d = 8.92 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$
$N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23} \text{ mol}^{-1}$
$a = 3.608 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Substituting the values into the formula:

$M = \dfrac{8.92 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \times (3.608 \times 10^{-8})^3}{4}$

$M = \dfrac{8.92 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 46.97 \times 10^{-24}}{4}$

$M = \dfrac{252.3}{4}$

$M \approx 63.07 \text{ g/mol}$

Rounding to the nearest integer, the atomic mass of the element is $63 \text{ g/mol}$.

Answer: $63$ g/mol

</details>

---

### Q50. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of unit cells present in $1$ g metal if the product of the density of metal and the volume of unit cell is $2.5 \times 10^{-22}$ g.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $2.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $3.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $4.0 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $4.0 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Density of the metal is given by $d = \dfrac{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}{\text{Volume of unit cell}}$.

Mass of one unit cell $= d \times V = 2.5 \times 10^{-22}$ g.

Number of unit cells in $1$ g of metal $= \dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}$.

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{1}{2.5 \times 10^{-22}} = 4.0 \times 10^{21}$.

Answer: $4.0 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q51. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An ionic compound is formed by elements X and Y. Element Y forms a face-centered cubic (FCC) lattice, and element X occupies one-third of the tetrahedral voids. What is the formula of the compound?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $X_2Y_3$
- **(B)** $XY$
- **(C)** $XY_3$
- **(D)** $X_2Y$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $X_2Y_3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Element Y forms a face-centered cubic (FCC) lattice.

Number of Y atoms per unit cell = $4$

Number of tetrahedral voids in an FCC unit cell = $2 \times 4 = 8$

Element X occupies one-third of the tetrahedral voids.

Number of X atoms per unit cell = $\dfrac{1}{3} \times 8 = \dfrac{8}{3}$

Ratio of X to Y = $\dfrac{8}{3} : 4 = 8 : 12 = 2 : 3$

The formula of the compound is $X_2Y_3$.

</details>

### Q52. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A metal crystallizes to form simple cubic unit cell. Calculate the void volume of unit cell. [$a = 3.36 \times 10^{-8}$ cm]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.807 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$.
- **(B)** $1.214 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$.
- **(C)** $3.662 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$.
- **(D)** $1.986 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.807 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$.**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a simple cubic unit cell, the relation between edge length $a$ and atomic radius $r$ is $a = 2r$.

The number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 1$.

Volume of the unit cell, $V = a^3 = (3.36 \times 10^{-8})^3 = 37.933 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3 = 3.7933 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$.

Volume occupied by the atom = $\dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3 = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi \left(\dfrac{a}{2}\right)^3 = \dfrac{\pi}{6}a^3$.

Packing fraction = $\dfrac{\text{Volume occupied}}{\text{Total volume}} = \dfrac{\pi}{6} \approx 0.5236$.

Fraction of void volume = $1 - 0.5236 = 0.4764$.

Void volume = $0.4764 \times V = 0.4764 \times 3.7933 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3 \approx 1.807 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$.

Answer: $1.807 \times 10^{-23}$ cm$^3$.

</details>

---

### Q53. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An element with a molar mass 27g mol$^{-1}$ forms a cubic unit cell. Calculate the number of atoms present in a unit cell if the density of metal is 2.7g cm$^{-3}$.
$[a^3 \times N_A = 40$ cm$^3$ mol$^{-1}]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1$
- **(B)** $2$
- **(C)** $4$
- **(D)** $6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a cubic unit cell is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

where $Z$ is the number of atoms per unit cell, $M$ is the molar mass, $a$ is the edge length, and $N_A$ is Avogadro's number.

Rearranging the formula to solve for $Z$:

$Z = \dfrac{d \times (a^3 \times N_A)}{M}$

Substituting the given values $d = 2.7$ g cm$^{-3}$, $M = 27$ g mol$^{-1}$, and $a^3 \times N_A = 40$ cm$^3$ mol$^{-1}$:

$Z = \dfrac{2.7 \times 40}{27}$

$Z = \dfrac{108}{27} = 4$

The number of atoms present in the unit cell is $4$.

</details>

---

### Q54. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A compound formed by elements A and B crystallizes in a cubic structure where A atoms are at the corner of the cube and B atoms are at the center of the cube. What is the formula of the compound?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\text{AB}$
- **(B)** $\text{A}_2\text{B}$
- **(C)** $\text{AB}_2$
- **(D)** $\text{A}_2\text{B}_2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\text{AB}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of A atoms per unit cell = $8 \times \dfrac{1}{8} = 1$

Number of B atoms per unit cell = $1 \times 1 = 1$

Ratio of A to B is $1:1$.

The formula of the compound is $\text{AB}$.

Answer: $\text{AB}$

</details>

### Q55. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a fcc arrangement of A and B atoms in which 'A' atoms are at the corners of the unit cell and 'B' atoms are at the face centers. One of the 'A' atoms is missing from one corner in unit cell. What is the simplest formula of a compound ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\text{A}_7\text{B}_8$
- **(B)** $\text{A}_7\text{B}_3$
- **(C)** $\text{AB}_3$
- **(D)** $\text{A}_7\text{B}_{24}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\text{A}_7\text{B}_{24}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In an fcc unit cell, there are $8$ corners and $6$ face centers.

Since one 'A' atom is missing from a corner, the number of 'A' atoms at the corners is $7$.

Contribution of each corner atom to the unit cell is $\dfrac{1}{8}$.

Effective number of 'A' atoms per unit cell = $7 \times \dfrac{1}{8} = \dfrac{7}{8}$

The 'B' atoms are at the $6$ face centers.

Contribution of each face center atom to the unit cell is $\dfrac{1}{2}$.

Effective number of 'B' atoms per unit cell = $6 \times \dfrac{1}{2} = 3$

Ratio of atoms A to B = $\dfrac{7}{8} : 3 = 7 : 24$

The simplest formula of the compound is $\text{A}_7\text{B}_{24}$.

Answer: $\text{A}_7\text{B}_{24}$

</details>

---

### Q56. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A compound forms bcc unit cell with edge length $400$ pm. Determine the molar mass of the compound if the density of compound is $3.5\ \text{g cm}^{-3}$ [$N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23}$]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $65.2\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $67.4\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $69.8\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $71.6\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $67.4\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The formula for the density of a unit cell is given by:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times a^3}$

For a bcc unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Given:
$d = 3.5\ \text{g cm}^{-3}$
$a = 400\ \text{pm} = 4 \times 10^{-8}\ \text{cm}$
$N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23}\ \text{mol}^{-1}$

Rearranging the formula to solve for molar mass $M$:

$M = \dfrac{d \times N_A \times a^3}{Z}$

Substituting the given values:

$M = \dfrac{3.5 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \times (4 \times 10^{-8})^3}{2}$

$M = \dfrac{3.5 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 64 \times 10^{-24}}{2}$

$M = \dfrac{3.5 \times 6.022 \times 6.4}{2}$

$M = 67.4464\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$

Rounding to one decimal place, $M \approx 67.4\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$.

Answer: $67.4\ \text{g mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q57. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the number of neighbouring spheres for any particle in the fcc crystal lattice ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6$
- **(B)** $8$
- **(C)** $10$
- **(D)** $12$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $12$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a face-centered cubic (fcc) crystal lattice, the coordination number is defined as the number of nearest neighbouring spheres for any given particle.

Considering a face-centered atom, it is in contact with $4$ corner atoms in its own plane. Additionally, it is in contact with $4$ face-centered atoms in the plane immediately above it and $4$ face-centered atoms in the plane immediately below it.

Total number of nearest neighbours $= 4 + 4 + 4 = 12$.

Thus, the coordination number for any particle in an fcc lattice is $12$.

Answer: $12$

</details>

---

### Q58. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Pt has FCC structure with an edge length of unit cell $392$ pm. What is the radius of Pt?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $138.6$ pm
- **(B)** $175.6$ pm
- **(C)** $185.6$ pm
- **(D)** $205.6$ pm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $138.6$ pm**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an FCC (face-centered cubic) structure, the relationship between the edge length $a$ and the atomic radius $r$ is given by:

$4r = \sqrt{2}a$

$r = \dfrac{a}{2\sqrt{2}}$

Given $a = 392$ pm:

$r = \dfrac{392}{2\sqrt{2}} = \dfrac{196}{\sqrt{2}}$

$r = 98\sqrt{2}$

Using $\sqrt{2} \approx 1.414$:

$r = 98 \times 1.414 = 138.57$ pm $\approx 138.6$ pm

Answer: $138.6$ pm

</details>

---

### Q59. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a cubic unit cell of an ionic compound, the eight corners are occupied by anions and cations at the center of the cube. Calculate the volume of the unit cell if density of the unit cell is $4 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$.
[Molar mass of compound $= 168.6$ g mol$^{-1}$ and $N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23}$]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.0 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$
- **(B)** $6.0 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$
- **(C)** $7.0 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$
- **(D)** $8.0 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $7.0 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of anions per unit cell $= 8 \times \dfrac{1}{8} = 1$

Number of cations per unit cell $= 1$

Total number of formula units per unit cell, $Z = 1$

The density of the unit cell is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times V}$

Rearranging for volume $V$:

$V = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times d}$

Substituting the given values:

$V = \dfrac{1 \times 168.6}{6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 4}$

$V = \dfrac{168.6}{24.088 \times 10^{23}}$

$V = 7.0 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Answer: $7.0 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

</details>

### Q60. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the total number of spheres that surrounds the tetrahedral hole in the close-packed three-dimensional structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$
- **(B)** $4$
- **(C)** $6$
- **(D)** $8$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

A tetrahedral void is formed when a sphere of the second layer is placed exactly over the triangular void formed by three spheres of the first layer.

Thus, a tetrahedral hole is surrounded by $4$ spheres ($3$ in one layer and $1$ in the adjacent layer).

The coordination number of a tetrahedral void is $4$.

Answer: $4$

</details>

### Q61. MHT CET 2025 (5 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In an ionic solid anions are arranged in hcp array and cations occupy $\frac{2}{3}$ of octahedral voids. What is the formula of ionic compound? [ consider $\mathrm{A}=$ cation ; $\mathrm{B}=$ anion]

**Options:**

- **(A)** AB
- **(B)** $\mathrm{A}_2 \mathrm{~B}_3$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{A}_3 \mathrm{~B}_2$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{AB}_3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{A}_2 \mathrm{~B}_3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the number of anions B in the hcp array be $N$.

The number of octahedral voids generated is equal to the number of atoms in the close-packed structure, which is $N$.

The cations A occupy $\dfrac{2}{3}$ of the octahedral voids.

Number of cations A = $\dfrac{2}{3} \times N$.

The ratio of the number of cations A to the number of anions B is $\dfrac{2}{3}N : N = 2 : 3$.

The formula of the ionic compound is $\mathrm{A}_2\mathrm{B}_3$.

Answer: $\mathrm{A}_2 \mathrm{~B}_3$

</details>

### Q62. MHT CET 2025 (5 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the edge length of unit cell if metal having atomic radius 170 pm forms simple cubic unit cell.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.17 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(B)** $3.40 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $5.12 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $6.81 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3.40 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a simple cubic unit cell, the relationship between the edge length $a$ and the atomic radius $r$ is given by:

$a = 2r$

Given $r = 170 \text{ pm}$, substituting this value yields:

$a = 2 \times 170 \text{ pm} = 340 \text{ pm}$

Converting picometers to centimeters using $1 \text{ pm} = 10^{-10} \text{ cm}$:

$a = 340 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm} = 3.40 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Answer: $3.40 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

</details>

### Q63. MHT CET 2025 (27 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$= 0.74 \times 5.2 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

$= 3.848 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $3.848 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

### Q64. MHT CET 2025 (27 Apr Shift 2)

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

Given that $\rho \times a^3 = 7.2 \times 10^{-22} \text{ g}$.

Number of atoms in $5.4 \text{ g}$ of the metal = $\dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one atom}}$

### Q65. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the radius of an atom of an element in pm if it forms bcc unit cell structure having edge length $4.3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 186.2
- **(B)** 215.0
- **(C)** 152.3
- **(D)** 282.8

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 186.2**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given edge length of the unit cell, $a = 4.3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{cm}$

Converting the edge length to pm:
$a = 4.3 \times 10^{-8} \times 10^{10} \mathrm{pm} = 430 \mathrm{pm}$

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, the relationship between the atomic radius $r$ and the edge length $a$ is:
$r = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}}{4}a$

Substituting the value of $a$:
$r = \dfrac{1.732}{4} \times 430$

$r = 0.433 \times 430 = 186.19 \mathrm{pm}$

Rounding off to one decimal place, we get $186.2 \mathrm{pm}$.

Answer: 186.2

</details>

### Q66. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of unit cells in $1 \mathrm{~cm}^3$ of an element if unit cell edge length is $2.0 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.78 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $2.61 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $1.25 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $4.61 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.25 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Volume of one unit cell is given by $V = a^3$

Substituting the given edge length $a = 2.0 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{cm}$:

$V = (2.0 \times 10^{-8})^3 = 8.0 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{cm}^3$

The number of unit cells in a given volume is the total volume divided by the volume of one unit cell.

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{1}{8.0 \times 10^{-24}}$

Number of unit cells $= 0.125 \times 10^{24} = 1.25 \times 10^{23}$

Answer: $1.25 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

---

### Q67. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the edge length of bcc unit cell if radius of a particle present in it is 186 pm .

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.296 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(B)** $7.301 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $3.715 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $5.419 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $4.296 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, the relationship between the edge length $a$ and the radius $r$ of the particle is given by:

$\sqrt{3}a = 4r$

$a = \dfrac{4r}{\sqrt{3}}$

Given $r = 186 \text{ pm}$, substituting this value:

$a = \dfrac{4 \times 186}{1.732} \text{ pm}$

$a = \dfrac{744}{1.732} \text{ pm} = 429.56 \text{ pm}$

Converting to cm:

$a = 429.56 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm} = 4.296 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Answer: $4.296 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

</details>

### Q68. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The compound forming ccp structure contains $9.6 \times 10^{23}$ atoms. Find the number of tetrahedral voids formed in it.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.00 \times 10^{24}$
- **(B)** $1.68 \times 10^{24}$
- **(C)** $1.92 \times 10^{24}$
- **(D)** $1.56 \times 10^{24}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.92 \times 10^{24}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of atoms in the ccp structure, $N = 9.6 \times 10^{23}$

In a close-packed structure, the number of tetrahedral voids is twice the number of atoms.

Number of tetrahedral voids $= 2N$

Number of tetrahedral voids $= 2 \times 9.6 \times 10^{23} = 19.2 \times 10^{23} = 1.92 \times 10^{24}$

Answer: $1.92 \times 10^{24}$

</details>

### Q69. MHT CET 2025 (26 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the coordination number of a particle in simple cubic structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 12
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 6
- **(D)** 8

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 6**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a simple cubic structure, each particle is located at the corners of the cubic unit cell.

Any given particle is surrounded by $6$ nearest neighbours (two along the x-axis, two along the y-axis, and two along the z-axis).

Therefore, the coordination number of a particle in a simple cubic structure is $6$.

Answer: 6

</details>

### Q70. MHT CET 2025 (25 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the total number of unit cells shared by each corner particle of bcc unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 8
- **(D)** 1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 8**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a three-dimensional crystal lattice, any corner of a cubic unit cell is shared by eight adjacent unit cells.

Four unit cells are in the same layer and four are in the layer directly above or below it.

Therefore, a particle located at the corner of a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell is shared by 8 unit cells, making its contribution to a single unit cell equal to $\dfrac{1}{8}$.

Answer: 8

</details>

### Q71. MHT CET 2025 (25 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the total volume occupied by all particles in fcc unit cell if volume of unit cell is $6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.321 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $4.350 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $5.126 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $4.736 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $4.736 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the packing efficiency is $74\%$.

This means that $74\%$ of the total volume of the unit cell is occupied by the particles.

Volume of the unit cell $= 6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Total volume occupied by particles $= \dfrac{74}{100} \times 6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

$= 0.74 \times 6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

$= 4.736 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $4.736 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q72. MHT CET 2025 (25 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of unit cells in $1 \mathrm{~cm}^3$ volume of metal if unit cell edge length is $1.25 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.40 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $3.35 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $5.12 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $2.25 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5.12 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Volume of one unit cell $V = a^3$

$V = (1.25 \times 10^{-8})^3 = \left(\dfrac{5}{4} \times 10^{-8}\right)^3 = \dfrac{125}{64} \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Number of unit cells in $1 \mathrm{~cm}^3 = \dfrac{\text{Total volume}}{\text{Volume of one unit cell}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{1}{\dfrac{125}{64} \times 10^{-24}} = \dfrac{64}{125} \times 10^{24} = 0.512 \times 10^{24} = 5.12 \times 10^{23}$

Answer: $5.12 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

---

### Q73. MHT CET 2025 (25 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the edge length of unit cell if a metal with atomic radius 128 pm forming fcc unit cell structure.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.62 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(B)** $2.56 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $2.96 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $3.12 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $3.62 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the relationship between the edge length $a$ and the atomic radius $r$ is given by:

$a = 2\sqrt{2}r$

Given $r = 128 \text{ pm}$.

$a = 2 \times 1.414 \times 128 \text{ pm}$

$a = 361.984 \text{ pm} \approx 362 \text{ pm}$

Converting the edge length into cm:

$a = 362 \times 10^{-12} \text{ m} = 362 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm} = 3.62 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Answer: $3.62 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

</details>

### Q74. MHT CET 2025 (25 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of particles present per unit cell if mass of a particle is $8.0 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~g}\left[\varrho \times a^3=3.2 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 4
- **(D)** 6

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 4**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by the formula:

$\rho = \dfrac{Z \times m}{a^3}$

where $Z$ is the number of particles per unit cell, $m$ is the mass of one particle, and $a^3$ is the volume of the unit cell.

Rearranging the formula to solve for $Z$, we get:

$Z = \dfrac{\rho \times a^3}{m}$

Substituting the given values:

$Z = \dfrac{3.2 \times 10^{-22}}{8.0 \times 10^{-23}}$

$Z = \dfrac{32 \times 10^{-23}}{8.0 \times 10^{-23}}$

$Z = 4$

The number of particles present per unit cell is $4$.

Answer: 4

</details>

### Q75. MHT CET 2025 (23 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of unit cells in 0.79 g metal if product of density and volume of unit cell is $1.58 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.96 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $1.72 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $4.46 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $5.0 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $5.0 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of one unit cell is given by the product of its density and volume.

Mass of one unit cell $= d \times a^3 = 1.58 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{g}$

Total mass of the metal $= 0.79 \mathrm{g}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{0.79}{1.58 \times 10^{-22}}$

Number of unit cells $= 0.5 \times 10^{22} = 5.0 \times 10^{21}$

Answer: $5.0 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q76. MHT CET 2025 (23 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume of bcc unit cell if radius of an atom present in it is $1.86 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.391 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $8.995 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $7.951 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $6.453 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $7.951 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, the relationship between the edge length $a$ and the atomic radius $r$ is:

$\sqrt{3}a = 4r$

$a = \dfrac{4r}{\sqrt{3}}$

Substituting $r = 1.86 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$ and $\sqrt{3} \approx 1.732$:

$a = \dfrac{4 \times 1.86 \times 10^{-8}}{1.732} \approx 4.30 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

The volume of the unit cell is $V = a^3$:

$V = (4.30 \times 10^{-8})^3$

$V = 79.507 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

$V \approx 7.951 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $7.951 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

### Q77. MHT CET 2025 (23 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume occupied by all particles in bcc unit cell if the volume of unit cell is $8.0 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.19 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $2.72 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $5.44 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $1.48 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5.44 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The packing efficiency of a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell is $68\%$.

This implies that $68\%$ of the total volume of the unit cell is occupied by the constituent particles.

Given the volume of the unit cell, $V = 8.0 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

Volume occupied by all particles = $\dfrac{68}{100} \times V$

Volume occupied by all particles = $0.68 \times 8.0 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3 = 5.44 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

Answer: $5.44 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

### Q78. MHT CET 2025 (23 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms in 1 g metal if it forms fcc crystal structure $\left[\varrho (\text{density}) \times \mathrm{a}^3=1.728 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.315 \times 10^{22}$
- **(B)** $3.156 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $4.108 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $1.452 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $2.315 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The mass of one unit cell is given by $\rho \times a^3 = 1.728 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{g}$.

For an fcc crystal structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

The number of unit cells in $1 \mathrm{g}$ of the metal is $\dfrac{1}{\rho \times a^3}$.

The total number of atoms in $1 \mathrm{g}$ of the metal is $Z \times \dfrac{1}{\rho \times a^3}$.

Substituting the given values:

Number of atoms $= \dfrac{4}{1.728 \times 10^{-22}}$

Number of atoms $= 2.3148 \times 10^{22} \approx 2.315 \times 10^{22}$

Answer: $2.315 \times 10^{22}$

</details>

### Q79. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 2)

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

The mass of one atom is $\dfrac{\rho \times a^3}{Z} = \dfrac{6.8 \times 10^{-22}}{4} = 1.7 \times 10^{-22} \text{ g}$.

The number of atoms in $1 \text{ g}$ of the element is $\dfrac{1}{1.7 \times 10^{-22}} = 5.882 \times 10^{21}$.

Answer: $5.882 \times 10^{21}$

### Q80. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume occupied by a particle in fcc unit cell if volume of unit cell is $1.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.44 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $2.96 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $8.37 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $6.15 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2.96 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The packing efficiency of a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell is $74\%$, meaning the particles occupy $0.74$ of the total volume.

Total volume occupied by all particles in the unit cell $= 0.74 \times V$

Substituting $V = 1.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$:

Total occupied volume $= 0.74 \times 1.6 \times 10^{-23} = 1.184 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

An fcc unit cell contains $4$ particles.

Volume occupied by one particle $= \dfrac{1.184 \times 10^{-23}}{4} = 0.296 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Volume occupied by one particle $= 2.96 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $2.96 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

### Q83. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms present per unit cell if product of density and volume of unit cell is $1.8 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}$
[Mass of an atom $=4.5 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~g}$]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1$
- **(B)** $2$
- **(C)** $4$
- **(D)** $6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by $d = \dfrac{Z \times m}{V}$, where $Z$ is the number of atoms per unit cell, $m$ is the mass of one atom, and $V$ is the volume of the unit cell.

Rearranging the formula, the mass of the unit cell is $d \times V = Z \times m$.

Given $d \times V = 1.8 \times 10^{-22} \text{ g}$ and $m = 4.5 \times 10^{-23} \text{ g}$.

Substituting the values:

$1.8 \times 10^{-22} = Z \times 4.5 \times 10^{-23}$

$Z = \dfrac{1.8 \times 10^{-22}}{4.5 \times 10^{-23}}$

$Z = \dfrac{18}{4.5} = 4$

Answer: $4$

</details>
