### Q84. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume of fcc unit cell in $\mathrm{cm}^3$ if void volume of it is $4.16 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.3 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(B)** $1.6 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(C)** $4.1 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(D)** $5.8 \times 10^{-23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.6 \times 10^{-23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The packing efficiency of an fcc unit cell is $74\%$.

The percentage of void space in an fcc unit cell is $100\% - 74\% = 26\%$.

Let the total volume of the unit cell be $V$.

Void volume $= 0.26 \times V$

Given void volume $= 4.16 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

$0.26 \times V = 4.16 \times 10^{-24}$

$V = \dfrac{4.16 \times 10^{-24}}{0.26}$

$V = 16 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

$V = 1.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $1.6 \times 10^{-23}$

</details>

### Q85. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the total number of tetrahedral and octahedral voids in 0.4 mol compound having such voids in it.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.4088 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $7.2264 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $4.8176 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $9.6352 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $7.2264 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of particles in $0.4$ mol of the compound is $N = 0.4 \times N_A$.

$N = 0.4 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} = 2.4088 \times 10^{23}$

In a close-packed structure, if the number of particles is $N$, the number of octahedral voids is $N$ and the number of tetrahedral voids is $2N$.

Total number of voids $= N + 2N = 3N$

Total number of voids $= 3 \times 2.4088 \times 10^{23} = 7.2264 \times 10^{23}$

Answer: $7.2264 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

### Q86. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The mass of an atom present in unit cell is $4.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~g}$ and the product of density and volume of unit cell is $1.792 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}$. What is the type of cubic unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Body centred unit cell
- **(B)** Face centred unit cell
- **(C)** Base centred unit cell
- **(D)** Simple cubic unit cell

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Face centred unit cell**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of one atom, $m = 4.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~g}$

Mass of the unit cell is given by the product of its density ($\rho$) and volume ($V$).

Mass of unit cell $= \rho \times V = 1.792 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}$

The number of atoms per unit cell ($Z$) is the ratio of the mass of the unit cell to the mass of one atom.

$Z = \dfrac{\text{Mass of unit cell}}{\text{Mass of one atom}}$

$Z = \dfrac{1.792 \times 10^{-22}}{4.4 \times 10^{-23}}$

$Z = \dfrac{17.92 \times 10^{-23}}{4.4 \times 10^{-23}} \approx 4$

Since the number of atoms per unit cell is $4$, the cubic unit cell is face-centred.

Answer: Face centred unit cell

</details>

### Q87. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of unit cells in $1 \mathrm{~cm}^3$ volume of an element if unit cell edge length is $4.0 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.63 \times 10^{22}$
- **(B)** $1.56 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $3.34 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $4.50 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.56 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Volume of one unit cell is given by $V = a^3$

Substituting the given edge length $a = 4.0 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{cm}$:

$V = (4.0 \times 10^{-8})^3 = 64 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{cm}^3$

Number of unit cells in $1 \mathrm{cm}^3$ volume is $\dfrac{\text{Total volume}}{\text{Volume of one unit cell}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{1}{64 \times 10^{-24}} = 0.015625 \times 10^{24} = 1.56 \times 10^{22}$

Answer: $1.56 \times 10^{22}$

</details>

---

### Q88. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the volume occupied by a particle in bcc unit cell if the volume of unit cell is $8.2 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.576 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $1.517 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $2.788 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $3.936 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2.788 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The packing efficiency of a bcc unit cell is $68\%$.

Total volume occupied by all particles in the unit cell = $\dfrac{68}{100} \times V$

Total volume = $0.68 \times 8.2 \times 10^{-23} = 5.576 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

The number of particles per unit cell in a bcc structure is $2$.

Volume occupied by a single particle = $\dfrac{5.576 \times 10^{-23}}{2} = 2.788 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $2.788 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

### Q89. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Calculate the molar mass of an element if it forms fcc unit cell structure
[mass of unit cell $=1.8 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}, \mathrm{~N}_{\mathrm{A}}=6.022 \times 10^{23} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $27.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $24.4 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $21.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $30.2 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $27.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an fcc unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

The mass of a unit cell is given by the formula:

$m = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A}$

where $M$ is the molar mass and $N_A$ is Avogadro's number.

Rearranging the formula to solve for $M$:

$M = \dfrac{m \times N_A}{Z}$

Substituting the given values:

$M = \dfrac{1.8 \times 10^{-22} \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}}{4}$

$M = \dfrac{1.8 \times 60.22}{4}$

$M = \dfrac{108.396}{4}$

$M = 27.099 \text{ g mol}^{-1} \approx 27.0 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $27.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q90. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the total number of tetrahedral and octahedral voids formed in 0.6 mol of a compound if it forms hcp structure.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.613 \times 10^{24}$
- **(B)** $7.226 \times 10^{24}$
- **(C)** $1.084 \times 10^{24}$
- **(D)** $2.913 \times 10^{24}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.084 \times 10^{24}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of atoms in $0.6$ mol of the compound is $N = 0.6 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} = 3.6132 \times 10^{23}$.

In a close-packed structure, the number of octahedral voids is equal to the number of atoms, $N$.

The number of tetrahedral voids is twice the number of atoms, $2N$.

Total number of voids $= N + 2N = 3N$.

Total number of voids $= 3 \times 3.6132 \times 10^{23} = 10.8396 \times 10^{23} = 1.084 \times 10^{24}$.

Answer: $1.084 \times 10^{24}$

</details>

### Q91. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following statements is NOT correct regarding voids in lattice structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Four spheres are involved in the formation of a tetrahedral void.
- **(B)** The octahedral void is surrounded by six spheres.
- **(C)** There are two tetrahedral voids associated with each atom.
- **(D)** There is one octahedral void associated with two atoms.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) There is one octahedral void associated with two atoms.**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a close-packed lattice structure, if the number of atoms or spheres is $N$, then the number of tetrahedral voids is $2N$ and the number of octahedral voids is $N$.

A tetrahedral void is formed by four spheres, and an octahedral void is formed by six spheres.

Since there are $N$ octahedral voids for $N$ atoms, there is exactly one octahedral void associated with each atom, not two atoms. Therefore, the statement that there is one octahedral void associated with two atoms is incorrect.

Answer: There is one octahedral void associated with two atoms.

</details>

### Q92. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In ionic solid, anions are arranged in ccp array and cations occupy $1 / 3$ tetrahedral voids. What is the formula of ionic compound?
[Consider $\mathrm{A}=$ cation $; \mathrm{B}=$ anion]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{AB}_3$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{A}_3 \mathrm{B}_2$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{A}_2 \mathrm{B}_3$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{AB}_4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{A}_2 \mathrm{B}_3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the number of anions (B) forming the ccp array be $N$.

The number of tetrahedral voids generated in a ccp arrangement is $2N$.

Since cations (A) occupy $\dfrac{1}{3}$ of the tetrahedral voids, the number of cations (A) is $\dfrac{1}{3} \times 2N = \dfrac{2N}{3}$.

The ratio of the number of cations (A) to the number of anions (B) is $\dfrac{2N}{3} : N = 2 : 3$.

Therefore, the formula of the ionic compound is $\mathrm{A}_2\mathrm{B}_3$.

Answer: $\mathrm{A}_2 \mathrm{B}_3$

</details>

### Q93. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The volume of simple unit cell is $x \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$. Calculate the value of $x$ if volume occupied by a particle in it is $2.1 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.0$
- **(B)** $3.5$
- **(C)** $4.0$
- **(D)** $4.5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $4.0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a simple cubic unit cell, the number of particles per unit cell is $1$.

The edge length $a$ and the radius of the particle $r$ are related by $a = 2r$.

Volume of the unit cell, $V = a^3 = (2r)^3 = 8r^3$.

Volume occupied by one particle, $v = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3$.

The ratio of the volume of the unit cell to the volume of the particle is:

$\dfrac{V}{v} = \dfrac{8r^3}{\dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3} = \dfrac{6}{\pi}$

Given that $v = 2.1 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$, we have:

$V = \dfrac{6}{\pi} \times 2.1 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

$V = \dfrac{6}{3.14} \times 2.1 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3 \approx 4.01 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Comparing this with $x \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$, we get $x \approx 4.0$.

Answer: $4.0$

</details>

---

### Q94. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms present in 1.58 g metal if it form bcc structure $\left[\varrho \times a^3=1.58 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.0 \times 10^{22}$
- **(B)** $2.0 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $3.0 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $4.0 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2.0 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of one unit cell is given by $\rho \times a^3 = 1.58 \times 10^{-22} \text{ g}$.

Number of unit cells in $1.58 \text{ g}$ of metal = $\dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one unit cell}} = \dfrac{1.58}{1.58 \times 10^{-22}} = 1.0 \times 10^{22}$.

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Total number of atoms = $Z \times \text{Number of unit cells} = 2 \times 1.0 \times 10^{22} = 2.0 \times 10^{22}$.

Answer: $2.0 \times 10^{22}$

</details>

### Q95. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume of unit cell having atomic radius 141.4 pm forming fcc unit cell.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $9.3 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $8.1 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $4.7 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an fcc unit cell, the edge length $a$ is related to the atomic radius $r$ by the formula:

$a = 2\sqrt{2}r$

Given $r = 141.4 \mathrm{~pm}$ and $\sqrt{2} \approx 1.414$, we can write $r \approx 100\sqrt{2} \mathrm{~pm}$.

Substituting the value of $r$:

$a = 2\sqrt{2} \times 100\sqrt{2} = 400 \mathrm{~pm} = 4 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

The volume of the unit cell $V$ is given by:

$V = a^3$

$V = (4 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm})^3 = 64 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3 = 6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q96. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the total number of particles present in basé centred unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** 6

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a base-centred (or end-centred) unit cell, particles are present at all $8$ corners and at the centres of $2$ opposite faces.

Contribution of particles at the corners $= 8 \times \dfrac{1}{8} = 1$

Contribution of particles at the face centres $= 2 \times \dfrac{1}{2} = 1$

Total number of particles in the unit cell $= 1 + 1 = 2$

Answer: 2

</details>

---

### Q97. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of unit cells in 0.4 g metal if the product of density and volume of unit cell is $1.2 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.1 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $2.2 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $3.3 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $4.4 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3.3 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The mass of one unit cell is given by the product of its density and volume.

Mass of one unit cell $= \rho \times V = 1.2 \times 10^{-22} \text{ g}$

Total mass of the metal $= 0.4 \text{ g}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{0.4}{1.2 \times 10^{-22}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{1}{3} \times 10^{22} = 3.33 \times 10^{21}$

Answer: $3.3 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q98. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

What is the total number of tetrahedral voids in 0.6 mole of compound that forms hep structure?

- **(B)** $7.2264 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $4.8396 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $5.0331 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $7.2264 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of atoms in $0.6$ mole of the compound is given by $N = 0.6 \times N_A$.

Substituting $N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23}$ mol$^{-1}$:

$N = 0.6 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} = 3.6132 \times 10^{23}$

In a close-packed structure (hcp), the number of tetrahedral voids is twice the number of atoms.

Number of tetrahedral voids $= 2N$

$= 2 \times 3.6132 \times 10^{23} = 7.2264 \times 10^{23}$

Answer: $7.2264 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

### Q99. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find the void volume of fcc unit cell in $\mathrm{cm}^3$ if the volume of fcc unit cell $1.25 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.25 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(B)** $2.16 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(C)** $1.34 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(D)** $4.20 \times 10^{-23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $3.25 \times 10^{-23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The packing efficiency of an fcc (face-centered cubic) unit cell is $74\%$.

The percentage of void space in the fcc unit cell is $100\% - 74\% = 26\%$.

Given the volume of the unit cell is $1.25 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

Void volume = $26\%$ of the volume of the unit cell

Void volume = $\dfrac{26}{100} \times 1.25 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Void volume = $0.26 \times 1.25 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3 = 0.325 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Void volume = $3.25 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $3.25 \times 10^{-23}$

</details>

### Q100. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of unit cells in 0.9 g metal if it forms bcc structure. $\left[\rho \times \mathrm{a}^3=3 \times 10^{-22}\right.$ gram $]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad 1.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $2.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $3.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $4.0 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3.0 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by the formula:

$\rho = \dfrac{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}{\text{Volume of one unit cell}} = \dfrac{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}{a^3}$

Rearranging this, the mass of one unit cell is:

$\text{Mass of one unit cell} = \rho \times a^3$

Given that $\rho \times a^3 = 3 \times 10^{-22}$ g, this is the mass of a single unit cell.

The total mass of the metal is $0.9$ g. The number of unit cells in the given mass can be calculated as:

$\text{Number of unit cells} = \dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}$

$\text{Number of unit cells} = \dfrac{0.9}{3 \times 10^{-22}}$

$\text{Number of unit cells} = 0.3 \times 10^{22} = 3.0 \times 10^{21}$

Answer: $3.0 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q101. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the volume of one particle in BCC structure if 'a' is edge length?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\pi \mathrm{a}^3}{3 \sqrt{2}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi \mathrm{a}^3}{12 \sqrt{2}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{16}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{8}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{16}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a body-centered cubic (BCC) structure, the particles touch each other along the body diagonal of the cube.

The length of the body diagonal is $\sqrt{3}a$, where $a$ is the edge length of the unit cell.

Since the body diagonal comprises the radii of two corner atoms and the diameter of the body-centered atom, its length is equal to $4r$, where $r$ is the radius of one particle.

$\sqrt{3}a = 4r \Rightarrow r = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}a}{4}$

Assuming the particle to be spherical, the volume of one particle is given by:

$V = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3$

Substituting the value of $r$:

$V = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi \left(\dfrac{\sqrt{3}a}{4}\right)^3$

$V = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi \left(\dfrac{3\sqrt{3}a^3}{64}\right)$

$V = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}\pi a^3}{16}$

Answer: $\frac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{16}$

</details>

---

### Q102. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Unit cell of an element has edge length of $5 Å$ with density $4 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$, if its atomic mass is 149 , identify the crystal structure.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Simple cubic close packed
- **(B)** Body centred cubic
- **(C)** Face centred cubic
- **(D)** Hexagonal close packed

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Body centred cubic**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given:

$a = 5 \text{ \AA} = 5 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

$\rho = 4 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$

$M = 149 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

$N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23} \text{ mol}^{-1}$

The density of the unit cell is given by the formula:

$\rho = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

Rearranging for the number of atoms per unit cell ($Z$):

$Z = \dfrac{\rho \times a^3 \times N_A}{M}$

Substituting the given values:

$Z = \dfrac{4 \times (5 \times 10^{-8})^3 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}}{149}$

$Z = \dfrac{4 \times 125 \times 10^{-24} \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}}{149}$

$Z = \dfrac{500 \times 10^{-1} \times 6.022}{149}$

$Z = \dfrac{301.1}{149} \approx 2$

Since $Z = 2$, the crystal structure is body centred cubic.

Answer: Body centred cubic

</details>

---

### Q103. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

In a solid, $\mathrm{B}^{-}$ions occupy corners and face centers of a cube forming ccp structure. If $A^{+}$ion occupy half the tetrahedral voids, formula of the solid is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{A}_2 \mathrm{~B}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{AB}_2$
- **(C)** $\quad A_2 B_3$
- **(D)** AB

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) AB**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a ccp structure, the number of $\mathrm{B}^{-}$ ions per unit cell is calculated from the corners and face centers:

Number of $\mathrm{B}^{-}$ ions = $8 \times \dfrac{1}{8} + 6 \times \dfrac{1}{2} = 1 + 3 = 4$

The number of tetrahedral voids in a ccp structure is twice the number of atoms per unit cell, which is $2 \times 4 = 8$.

Since $\mathrm{A}^{+}$ ions occupy half of the tetrahedral voids:

Number of $\mathrm{A}^{+}$ ions = $\dfrac{1}{2} \times 8 = 4$

The ratio of $\mathrm{A}^{+}$ to $\mathrm{B}^{-}$ is $4 : 4$, which simplifies to $1 : 1$.

Therefore, the formula of the solid is AB.

Answer: AB

</details>

### Q104. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the total volume occupied by all atoms in simple cubic unit cell if radius of atom is $3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$ :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.13 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $2.25 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $3.15 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $4.37 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.13 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a simple cubic unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 1$.

The volume of one spherical atom is given by $V = \dfrac{4}{3} \pi r^3$.

Total volume occupied by all atoms in the unit cell $= Z \times \dfrac{4}{3} \pi r^3$.

Substituting the given radius $r = 3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$:

Total volume $= 1 \times \dfrac{4}{3} \times 3.14 \times (3 \times 10^{-8})^3$

Total volume $= \dfrac{4}{3} \times 3.14 \times 27 \times 10^{-24}$

Total volume $= 36 \times 3.14 \times 10^{-24}$

Total volume $= 113.04 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Total volume $= 1.13 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $1.13 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q105. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A metal crystallises in bcc structure with edge length $4 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$. If density of unit cell is $10 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$. What is its molar mass?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $152 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $120 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $193 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $193 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a bcc unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

The formula for the density of a unit cell is:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

Rearranging to solve for molar mass $M$:

$M = \dfrac{d \times a^3 \times N_A}{Z}$

Substituting the given values ($d = 10 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$, $a = 4 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$, $N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$):

$M = \dfrac{10 \times (4 \times 10^{-8})^3 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}}{2}$

$M = \dfrac{10 \times 64 \times 10^{-24} \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}}{2}$

$M = \dfrac{640 \times 10^{-24} \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}}{2}$

$M = \dfrac{64 \times 6.022}{2}$

$M = 32 \times 6.022 = 192.704 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

Rounding off to the nearest integer, we get $193 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

Answer: $193 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

---

### Q106. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is atomic radius of an element if it crystallises in BCC structure with edge length of unit cell 287 pm ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 124 pm
- **(B)** 131 pm
- **(C)** 137 pm
- **(D)** 174 pm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 124 pm**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (BCC) unit cell, the relationship between the atomic radius $r$ and the edge length $a$ is given by:

$r = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}}{4}a$

Given $a = 287$ pm.

Substituting the value of $a$:

$r = \dfrac{1.732}{4} \times 287$

$r = 0.433 \times 287$

$r = 124.27$ pm

Rounding to the nearest integer, the atomic radius is $124$ pm.

Answer: 124 pm

</details>

### Q107. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

What type of unit cell from following is common to all seven types of crystal systems?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Simple
- **(B)** Body-centred
- **(C)** Face-centred
- **(D)** Base-centred

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Simple**

**Detailed Solution:**

The seven crystal systems are cubic, tetragonal, orthorhombic, monoclinic, hexagonal, rhombohedral, and triclinic.

The simple (primitive) unit cell is the only type of unit cell that is present in all seven crystal systems. Other variations like body-centred, face-centred, and base-centred are found only in specific crystal systems.

Answer: Simple

### Q108. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the number of octahedral voids present in 0.2 mol of a compound forming hep structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.204 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $2.387 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $3.011 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $3.321 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.204 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of moles of the compound = $0.2 \text{ mol}$

Number of atoms in the hcp structure = $0.2 \times N_A$

$= 0.2 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} = 1.2044 \times 10^{23}$

In any close-packed structure, the number of octahedral voids is equal to the number of atoms constituting the lattice.

Number of octahedral voids = $1.204 \times 10^{23}$

Answer: $1.204 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

---

### Q109. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Metallic silver has fcc structure. If radius of Ag atom is 144 pm . What is the edge length of unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.07 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(B)** $3.22 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $2.63 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $\quad .2 .23 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $4.07 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an fcc unit cell, the relationship between edge length $a$ and atomic radius $r$ is given by:

$a = 2\sqrt{2}r$

Substituting $r = 144 \text{ pm}$:

$a = 2 \times 1.414 \times 144 \text{ pm}$

$a = 407.232 \text{ pm}$

Converting to cm:

$a = 407.232 \times 10^{-12} \text{ m} = 407.232 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm}$

$a = 4.07 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Answer: $4.07 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

</details>

---

### Q110. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is minimum number of spheres required to develop a tetrahedral void?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 6
- **(D)** 3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 4**

**Detailed Solution:**

A tetrahedral void is formed when a sphere of the second layer is placed above the triangular void of the first layer, which is formed by three spheres in contact.

Thus, a tetrahedral void is surrounded by four spheres.

The minimum number of spheres required to develop a tetrahedral void is $4$.

Answer: 4

</details>

### Q111. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the density of a metal having molar mass $197 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ if it forms fcc structure. $\left[\mathrm{a}^3 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=40 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $23.5 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$
- **(B)** $21.2 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$
- **(C)** $17.5 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$
- **(D)** $19.7 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $19.7 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an fcc structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

The density $d$ of the crystal is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

Substituting the given values $M = 197 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$ and $a^3 \times N_A = 40 \text{ cm}^3 \text{ mol}^{-1}$:

$d = \dfrac{4 \times 197}{40}$

$d = \dfrac{197}{10}$

$d = 19.7 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$

Answer: $19.7 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$

</details>

---

### Q112. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the volume of simple cubic unit cell if the radius of particle in it is 400 pm .

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.36 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $5.12 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $5.84 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $6.60 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $5.12 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a simple cubic unit cell, the edge length $a$ and the radius of the particle $r$ are related by $a = 2r$.

Given $r = 400 \mathrm{~pm} = 400 \times 10^{-10} \mathrm{~cm} = 4 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$.

Substituting the value of $r$:

$a = 2 \times 4 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm} = 8 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

The volume of the unit cell is given by $V = a^3$.

$V = (8 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm})^3$

$V = 512 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3 = 5.12 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $5.12 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q113. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate number of atoms per unit cell of an element having molar mass $92.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ and density $8.6 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ forming cubic unit cell structure. $\left[\mathrm{a}^3 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=21.5 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 3
- **(D)** 4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a cubic unit cell is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

Where:
$d$ is the density of the unit cell
$Z$ is the number of atoms per unit cell
$M$ is the molar mass
$a^3$ is the volume of the unit cell
$N_A$ is Avogadro's number

Rearranging the formula to solve for $Z$:

$Z = \dfrac{d \times (a^3 \times N_A)}{M}$

Substituting the given values ($d = 8.6 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$, $a^3 \times N_A = 21.5 \text{ cm}^3 \text{ mol}^{-1}$, $M = 92.0 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$):

$Z = \dfrac{8.6 \times 21.5}{92.0}$

$Z = \dfrac{184.9}{92.0}$

$Z \approx 2$

The number of atoms per unit cell is $2$, which corresponds to a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure.

Answer: 2

</details>

---

### Q114. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the volume of unit cell if an element having molar mass $92 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ that forms bcc structure $\left[\varrho \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=5.0 \times 10^{24} \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.44 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $5.86 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $3.68 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $4.76 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3.68 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by the formula:

$\rho = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times V}$

Rearranging for the volume of the unit cell $V$:

$V = \dfrac{Z \times M}{\rho \times N_A}$

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Given values are:
$M = 92 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$
$\rho \times N_A = 5.0 \times 10^{24} \text{ g cm}^{-3} \text{ mol}^{-1}$

Substituting these values into the formula:

$V = \dfrac{2 \times 92}{5.0 \times 10^{24}}$

$V = \dfrac{184}{5.0 \times 10^{24}}$

$V = 36.8 \times 10^{-24} \text{ cm}^3$

$V = 3.68 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Answer: $3.68 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q115. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the volume of fcc unit cell if radius of a particle in it is 106.05 pm.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad 7.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $9.9 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $2.7 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $6.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2.7 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the relationship between the edge length $a$ and the radius $r$ of the particle is given by:

$a = 2\sqrt{2}r$

Substituting the given radius $r = 106.05 \mathrm{\ pm}$:

$a = 2 \times 1.414 \times 106.05 \mathrm{\ pm} \approx 300 \mathrm{\ pm}$

Converting the edge length to centimeters:

$a = 300 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{\ m} = 300 \times 10^{-10} \mathrm{\ cm} = 3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{\ cm}$

The volume of the unit cell is $V = a^3$:

$V = (3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{\ cm})^3$

$V = 27 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{\ cm}^3 = 2.7 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{\ cm}^3$

Answer: $2.7 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

### Q116. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the coordination number of a particle in fcc structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 12
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 4
- **(D)** 6

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 12**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a face-centered cubic (fcc) structure, each constituent particle is surrounded by $12$ nearest neighbours.

For a particle at the face centre, it is in contact with $4$ corner atoms in its own plane, $4$ face-centred atoms in the plane above it, and $4$ face-centred atoms in the plane below it.

Thus, the coordination number of a particle in an fcc structure is $12$.

Answer: 12

</details>

### Q117. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the molar mass of an element having density $5.6 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ that forms bcc structure. $\left[\mathrm{a}^3 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=75 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $198 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $210 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $118 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $225 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $210 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) lattice, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

The density of the unit cell is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

Rearranging the formula to solve for molar mass $M$:

$M = \dfrac{d \times (a^3 \times N_A)}{Z}$

Substituting the given values $d = 5.6 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$, $a^3 \times N_A = 75 \text{ cm}^3 \text{ mol}^{-1}$, and $Z = 2$:

$M = \dfrac{5.6 \times 75}{2}$

$M = 2.8 \times 75 = 210 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $210 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

---

### Q118. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the total number of atoms present in fcc unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 6
- **(D)** 1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 4**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, atoms are present at the $8$ corners and at the center of all the $6$ faces of the cube.

Contribution of atoms at the corners $= 8 \times \dfrac{1}{8} = 1$

Contribution of atoms at the face centers $= 6 \times \dfrac{1}{2} = 3$

Total number of atoms in an fcc unit cell $= 1 + 3 = 4$

Answer: 4

</details>

---

### Q119. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the relation between edge length and total volume occupied by atoms in bec unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{V}=\dfrac{\pi \mathrm{a}^3}{6}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{V}=\dfrac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{8}$
- **(C)** $\quad \mathrm{V}=\dfrac{\pi \mathrm{a}^3}{3 \sqrt{2}}$
- **(D)** $\quad \mathrm{V}=\dfrac{\pi \mathrm{a}^3}{16}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{V}=\dfrac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{8}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a bcc unit cell, the atoms touch along the body diagonal.

Length of body diagonal = $\sqrt{3}a$

Since the body diagonal comprises the radii of two corner atoms and the diameter of the body-centered atom, we have $4r = \sqrt{3}a$, which gives $r = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}a}{4}$.

The number of effective atoms in a bcc unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Total volume occupied by atoms is $V = Z \times \dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3$.

Substituting the value of $r$:

$V = 2 \times \dfrac{4}{3}\pi \left(\dfrac{\sqrt{3}a}{4}\right)^3$

$V = \dfrac{8}{3}\pi \left(\dfrac{3\sqrt{3}a^3}{64}\right)$

$V = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}\pi a^3}{8}$

Answer: $\mathrm{V}=\dfrac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{8}$

</details>

---

### Q120. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume of unit cell when metal having density $1 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ and molar mass $23 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ crystallises to form bce structure.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.0 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $8.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $\quad 9.5 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $7.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $7.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by the formula:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times V}$

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Given values are:
$d = 1 \mathrm{\ g \ cm^{-3}}$
$M = 23 \mathrm{\ g \ mol^{-1}}$
$N_A = 6.022 \times 10^{23} \mathrm{\ mol^{-1}}$

Rearranging the formula to find the volume $V$ of the unit cell:

$V = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times d}$

Substituting the given values:

$V = \dfrac{2 \times 23}{6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 1}$

$V = \dfrac{46}{6.022 \times 10^{23}}$

$V \approx 7.638 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{\ cm^3}$

Rounding to one decimal place, the volume is $7.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{\ cm^3}$.

Answer: $7.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

### Q121. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the total number of different types of unit cells present in triclinic crystal system?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 3
- **(D)** 4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 1**

**Detailed Solution:**

The triclinic crystal system is the most unsymmetrical crystal system with edge lengths $a \neq b \neq c$ and axial angles $\alpha \neq \beta \neq \gamma \neq 90^{\circ}$.

According to the Bravais lattices, the triclinic crystal system possesses only one type of unit cell, which is the primitive (or simple) unit cell.

Therefore, the total number of different types of unit cells present in the triclinic crystal system is $1$.

Answer: 1

</details>

---

### Q122. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Calculate the void volume of simple cubic unit cell if the volume of unit cell is $5.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.435 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $1.761 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $2.619 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $3.880 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2.619 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a simple cubic unit cell, the relation between the edge length $a$ and the radius of the atom $r$ is $a = 2r$.

The packing fraction is given by the ratio of the volume occupied by the atoms to the total volume of the unit cell:

$\text{Packing fraction} = \dfrac{\dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3}{a^3} = \dfrac{\dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3}{(2r)^3} = \dfrac{\pi}{6}$

Taking $\pi = \dfrac{22}{7}$, the packing fraction is $\dfrac{22}{42} = \dfrac{11}{21}$.

The fraction of void volume in the unit cell is:

$\text{Void fraction} = 1 - \dfrac{11}{21} = \dfrac{10}{21}$

Given the total volume of the unit cell is $5.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$, the void volume is:

$\text{Void volume} = \dfrac{10}{21} \times 5.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3 = 2.619 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $2.619 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q123. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the radius of metal atom if it forms bcc unit cell having edge length 530 pm .

**Options:**

- **(A)** 229.5 pm
- **(B)** 187.4 pm
- **(C)** 459.0 pm
- **(D)** 265.2 pm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 229.5 pm**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, the atoms touch each other along the body diagonal.

The length of the body diagonal is $\sqrt{3}a$, where $a$ is the edge length of the unit cell.

Since the body diagonal contains two full atomic radii from the center atom and one radius from each of the two corner atoms, its length is equal to $4r$.

$4r = \sqrt{3}a$

$r = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}}{4}a$

Given $a = 530$ pm.

$r = \dfrac{1.732}{4} \times 530$

$r = 0.433 \times 530$

$r = 229.49$ pm $\approx 229.5$ pm.

Answer: 229.5 pm

</details>

### Q124. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate molar mass of an element having density $8.6 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ if it forms bcc structure $\left[\mathrm{a}^3 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=22.0 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad 106.18 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $94.6 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $88.25 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $80.16 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $94.6 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The formula for the density of a unit cell is given by $d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 2$.

Given:
$d = 8.6 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$
$a^3 \times N_A = 22.0 \text{ cm}^3 \text{ mol}^{-1}$

Substituting the values into the formula:
$8.6 = \dfrac{2 \times M}{22.0}$

$M = \dfrac{8.6 \times 22.0}{2}$

$M = 8.6 \times 11.0 = 94.6 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $94.6 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q125. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the volume of unit cell of an element having molar mass $63.5 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. that forms fce structure $\left[\varrho \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=5.5 \times 10^{24} \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.1 .02 \times 10^{-25} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $5.430 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $5.014 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $4.618 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $4.618 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by:

$\rho = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times V}$

Rearranging for the volume of the unit cell $V$:

$V = \dfrac{Z \times M}{\rho \times N_A}$

For an fcc structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

Substituting the given values $M = 63.5 \mathrm{~g~mol}^{-1}$ and $\rho \times N_A = 5.5 \times 10^{24} \mathrm{~g~cm}^{-3} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$:

$V = \dfrac{4 \times 63.5}{5.5 \times 10^{24}}$

$V = \dfrac{254}{5.5 \times 10^{24}}$

$V = 46.18 \times 10^{-24} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

$V = 4.618 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $4.618 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

### Q126. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find the void volume of bec unit cell in $\mathrm{cm}^3$ if volume of unit cell is $1.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.8 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(B)** $3.6 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(C)** $2.4 \times 10^{-23}$
- **(D)** $1.2 \times 10^{-23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $4.8 \times 10^{-23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, the packing efficiency is $68\%$.

The percentage of empty space (void volume) in the unit cell is $100\% - 68\% = 32\%$.

Given the total volume of the unit cell is $V = 1.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

Void volume = $32\%$ of $V = \dfrac{32}{100} \times 1.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

Void volume = $0.32 \times 1.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3 = 0.48 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3 = 4.8 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

Answer: $4.8 \times 10^{-23}$

</details>

---

### Q127. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume occupied by all atoms in bcc unit cell if the volume of unit cell is $1.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.40 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $3.51 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $1.56 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $1.02 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.02 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, the relation between the atomic radius $r$ and the edge length $a$ is $4r = \sqrt{3}a$.

The number of atoms per unit cell in a bcc structure is $Z = 2$.

The packing fraction is the ratio of the volume occupied by the atoms to the total volume of the unit cell:

$\text{Packing fraction} = \dfrac{2 \times \dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3}{a^3} = \dfrac{\sqrt{3}\pi}{8} \approx 0.68$

Given the total volume of the unit cell $V = 1.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$.

The volume occupied by all atoms is:

$V_{\text{atoms}} = 0.68 \times 1.5 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3 = 1.02 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

Answer: $1.02 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q128. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the coordination number of a particle in hcp structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 6
- **(D)** 12

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 12**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a hexagonal close-packed (hcp) structure, each constituent particle is in direct contact with $6$ neighboring particles in its own layer.

Additionally, it touches $3$ particles in the layer immediately above it and $3$ particles in the layer immediately below it.

Therefore, the total coordination number of a particle in an hcp structure is $6 + 3 + 3 = 12$.

Answer: 12

</details>

---

### Q129. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the density of an element having molar mass $63 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ that forms FCC structure $\left[\mathrm{a}^3 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=28 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$
- **(B)** $\quad 9.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$
- **(C)** $5.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$
- **(D)** $7.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\quad 9.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The formula for the density of a unit cell is given by:

$d = \dfrac{Z \times M}{a^3 \times N_A}$

For a face-centered cubic (FCC) structure, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

Given:
$M = 63 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$
$a^3 \times N_A = 28 \text{ cm}^3 \text{ mol}^{-1}$

Substituting the given values into the density formula:

$d = \dfrac{4 \times 63}{28}$

$d = \dfrac{252}{28}$

$d = 9.0 \text{ g cm}^{-3}$

Answer: $\quad 9.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$

</details>

---

### Q130. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of unit cells in 10.8 g metal $\left(\varrho a^3=7.2 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.5 \times 10^{22}$
- **(B)** $3.0 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $4.5 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $6.0 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.5 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The mass of one unit cell is given by the product of its density and volume, which is $\rho a^3$.

Mass of one unit cell $= 7.2 \times 10^{-22}$ g

Total mass of the metal $= 10.8$ g

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{\text{Total mass}}{\text{Mass of one unit cell}}$

Number of unit cells $= \dfrac{10.8}{7.2 \times 10^{-22}}$

Number of unit cells $= 1.5 \times 10^{22}$

Answer: $1.5 \times 10^{22}$

</details>

### Q131. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the radius of an atom of metal if it forms simple cubic unit cell with edge length 380 pm .

**Options:**

- **(A)** 285.0 pm
- **(B)** 67.2 pm
- **(C)** 190.0 pm
- **(D)** 82.3 pm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 190.0 pm**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a simple cubic unit cell, the atoms touch each other along the edges of the cube.

The relationship between the edge length $a$ and the radius of the atom $r$ is given by:

$a = 2r$

Given that the edge length $a = 380 \text{ pm}$.

Substituting the value of $a$:

$r = \dfrac{a}{2} = \dfrac{380}{2} = 190 \text{ pm}$

Answer: 190.0 pm

</details>

---

### Q132. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the edge length of fcc unit cell if radius of metal atom is 139 pm .

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.78 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(B)** $3.21 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $3.93 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $6.95 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3.93 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the relationship between the edge length $a$ and the atomic radius $r$ is given by:

$a = 2\sqrt{2}r$

Given $r = 139 \text{ pm}$:

$a = 2 \times 1.414 \times 139 \text{ pm}$

$a = 393.1 \text{ pm}$

Converting picometers to centimeters:

$a = 393.1 \times 10^{-10} \text{ cm}$

$a = 3.93 \times 10^{-8} \text{ cm}$

Answer: $3.93 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

</details>

---

### Q133. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Calculate the volume of unit cell of an element having molar mass $27 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ that forms fce unit cell. $\left[\rho . \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=16.0 \times 10^{23} \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$
[Note: The question has been modified to get the correct answer.]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $7.50 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $6.75 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $5.75 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $8.25 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $6.75 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The density of a unit cell is given by the formula:

$\rho = \dfrac{Z \times M}{N_A \times V}$

For a face-centered cubic (fcc) unit cell, the number of atoms per unit cell is $Z = 4$.

Rearranging the formula to solve for the volume $V$ of the unit cell:

$V = \dfrac{Z \times M}{\rho \times N_A}$

Substituting the given values $M = 27 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$ and $\rho \times N_A = 16.0 \times 10^{23} \text{ g cm}^{-3} \text{ mol}^{-1}$:

$V = \dfrac{4 \times 27}{16.0 \times 10^{23}}$

$V = \dfrac{108}{16.0 \times 10^{23}}$

$V = 6.75 \times 10^{-23} \text{ cm}^3$

Answer: $6.75 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

</details>

---

### Q134. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the total number of particles present in bcc unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 3
- **(D)** 4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a body-centered cubic (bcc) unit cell, particles are located at the corners and at the body center.

Number of particles at the corners = $8$

Contribution of each corner particle to the unit cell = $\dfrac{1}{8}$

Total contribution from corners = $8 \times \dfrac{1}{8} = 1$

Number of particles at the body center = $1$

Contribution of the body center particle to the unit cell = $1$

Total number of particles in a bcc unit cell = $1 + 1 = 2$

Answer: 2

</details>

---

Calculate the density of metal having molar mass $210 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ that forms simple cubic unit cell. $\left(\mathrm{a}^3 \cdot \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=21.5 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

### Q136. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the edge length of bcc unit cell if radius of metal atom is $227 \mathrm{pm}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.54 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(B)** $5.24 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $6.42 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $1.135 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $5.24 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For bcc unit cell, $r=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{4} \mathrm{a}$
$\begin{aligned}
\therefore \quad \mathrm{a}=\frac{4 \mathrm{r}}{\sqrt{3}}=\frac{4 \times 227}{1.73} & =524.85 \mathrm{pm} \\
& =524.85 \times 10^{-10} \mathrm{~cm} \\
& =5.24 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q137. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In an ionic crystalline solid, atoms of element $Y$ forms hep structure. The atoms of element $X$ occupy one third of tetrahedral voids. What is the formula of compound?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{X}_2 \mathrm{Y}_3$
- **(B)** $X Y$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{X}_3 \mathrm{Y}_3$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{XY}_2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{X}_2 \mathrm{Y}_3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The atoms of element $\mathrm{Y}$ form hcp structure. The number of tetrahedral voids generated is twice the number of $\mathrm{Y}$ atoms. Thus, number of tetrahedral voids $=2 \mathrm{Y}$ The atoms of element $X$ occupy (1/3) of these tetrahedral voids.
Hence, number of $\mathrm{X}$ atoms $=2 \mathrm{Y} \times 1 / 3=2 / 3 \mathrm{Y}$ Ratio of $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}$ atoms $=2 / 3 \mathrm{Y}: 1 \mathrm{Y}$ $=2 / 3: 1=2: 3$
$\therefore \quad$ Formula of compound $=\mathrm{X}_2 \mathrm{Y}_3$

</details>

### Q138. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the volume of unit cell if an element having molar mass $56 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ that forms bcc unit cells.
$$
\left[\rho . \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=4: 8 \times 10^{24} \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]
$$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.17 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $4.79 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $3.31 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $2.33 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2.33 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Density of unit cell }=\rho=\frac{M n}{a^3 N_A} \\ & \text { Volume of unit cell }\left(a^3\right)=\frac{M n}{\rho N_A} \\ & =\frac{56 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \times 2}{4.8 \times 10^{24} \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}} \\ & =2.33 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3 \\ & \end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q144. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

- **(B)** $2.1 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$

### Q146. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What are the number of octahedral and tetrahedral voids in 0.3 mole substance respectively if it forms hcp structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.8066 \times 10^{23}$ and $3.6132 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $3.6132 \times 10^{23}$ and $1.8066 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $6.022 \times 10^{23}$ and $12.044 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $12.044 \times 10^{23}$ and $6.022 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.8066 \times 10^{23}$ and $3.6132 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of atoms in $0.3 \mathrm{~mol}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =0.3 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}} \\
& =0.3 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \\
& =1.8066 \times 10^{23}
\end{aligned}
$$
i. For hep structure, Number of octahedral voids
$=$ Number of atoms
$$
=1.8066 \times 10^{23}
$$
ii. For hcp structure, Number of tetrahedral voids
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =2 \times \text { Number of atoms } \\
& =2 \times 1.8066 \times 10^{23} \\
& =3.6132 \times 10^{23}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q149. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the number of unit cells present in a cubic pack crystal lattice having 4 atoms per unit cell and weighing $0.60 \mathrm{~g}$ (molar mass $\left.60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right) ?$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $1.5 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $3.0 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $6.0 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.5 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of moles $=\frac{0.6 \mathrm{~g}}{60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}}=0.01 \mathrm{~mol}$
Total number of atoms $=0.01 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}$
Number of atoms per unit cell $=4$ (Given)
$\therefore \quad$ Number of unit cells present in the given cubic crystal lattice
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\frac{0.01 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}}{4} \\
& =1.5 \times 10^{21} \text { unit cells }
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q150. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is atomic mass of an element with $\mathrm{BCC}$ structure and density $10 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ having edge length $300 \mathrm{pm}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $51.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $60.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $81.3 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $96.8 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $96.8 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For bcc unit cell, $\mathrm{n}=2$.
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { Density of bcc unit cell }=\rho=\frac{\mathrm{Mn}}{\mathrm{a}^3 \mathrm{~N}_{\mathrm{A}}} \\
& \mathrm{M}=\frac{\rho \times \mathrm{a}^3 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}}{\mathrm{n}} \\
& \mathrm{M}=\frac{10 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3} \times\left(3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}\right)^3 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \mathrm{atom} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}}{2 \text { atoms }} \\
& \mathrm{M}=81.3 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q151. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which formula is used to calculate edge length in bcc structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $a=\frac{\sqrt{3} r}{4}$
- **(B)** $a=\frac{4}{\sqrt{3} r}$
- **(C)** $a=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{4 r}$
- **(D)** $a=\frac{4 r}{\sqrt{3}}$

### Q152. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find the radius of an atom in fcc unit cell having edge length $393 \mathrm{pm}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $196.51 \mathrm{pm}$
- **(B)** $170.22 \mathrm{pm}$
- **(C)** 78.63 $\mathrm{pm}$
- **(D)** $138.93 \mathrm{pm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $138.93 \mathrm{pm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For foc crystal structure,
$\begin{aligned}
4 \mathrm{r} & =\sqrt{2} \mathrm{a} \\
\therefore \quad \mathrm{r} & =\frac{\sqrt{2} \mathrm{a}}{4}=\frac{1.414 \times 393}{4}=138.93 \mathrm{pm}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q153. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the molar mass of an element with density $2.7 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ that forms fcc structure.
$\left[\mathrm{a}^3 \cdot \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=40 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $112 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $54 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $27 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $78 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $27 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For fcc unit cell, $n=4$.
$\begin{aligned}
& \text { Density }(\rho)=\frac{M \text { n }}{a^3 N_A} \\
& 2.7=\frac{M \times 4}{40} \\
& M=\frac{2.7 \times 40}{4}=27 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q154. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the edge length of unit cell of metal which crystallises to bcc structure.
(Radius of metal atom $=173 \mathrm{pm}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.01 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(B)** $4.00 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $4.5 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $5.5 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4.00 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For bcc unit cell, $r=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{4} \mathrm{a}$
$\begin{aligned}
\therefore \quad \mathrm{a}=\frac{4 \mathrm{r}}{\sqrt{3}}=\frac{4 \times 173}{1.73} & =400 \mathrm{pm} \\
& =400 \times 10^{-10} \mathrm{~cm} \\
& =4.00 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q157. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the molar mass of an element having density $21 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ that forms fcc unit cell $\left[\mathrm{a}^3 \cdot \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=36 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad 292.00 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $189.00 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $140.00 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $108.00 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $189.00 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \operatorname{Density}(\rho)=\frac{M \text { n }}{a^3 N_A} \\ & \therefore \quad 21 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}=\frac{\mathrm{M} \times 4}{36 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}} \\ & \therefore \quad \mathrm{M}=\frac{21 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3} \times 36 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}}{4} \\ & =189.00 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\ & \end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q158. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

Number of atoms in one mole of atom of a metal $=6.022 \times 10^{23}$

</details>

### Q162. MHT CET 2022 (11 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the mass of bcc unit cell if metal has molar mass $56 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.86 \times 10^{-22}$ gram
- **(B)** $9.3 \times 10^{-24}$ gram
- **(C)** $2.79 \times 10^{-24}$ gram
- **(D)** $3.72 \times 10^{-22}$ gram

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.86 \times 10^{-22}$ gram**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Mass of an atom }=\frac{56}{6.02 \times 10^{23}} \mathrm{~g} \\ & \text { Mass of unit cell }=\frac{2 \times 56}{6.02 \times 10^{23}} \\ & =18.6 \times 10^{-23} \\ & \text { or } 1.86 \times 10^{-22} \mathrm{~g}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q163. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 2)

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

### Q164. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the number of atoms in 5 gram metal that crystallises to form simple cubic unit cell structure having edge length $336 \mathrm{pm}$. (Density of metal $=9.4 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.4 \times 10^{22}$
- **(B)** $1.8 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $1.0 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $2.1 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.4 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{d}=\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{v}} \times \frac{\mathrm{M}}{\mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}} \\ & 9.4=\frac{1 \times \mathrm{M}}{\left(3.36 \times 10^{-8}\right)^3 \times 6.02 \times 10^{23}}\end{aligned}$
$\mathrm{M}=214.65 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{mol}$
No. of atoms $=\frac{5}{214.65} \times 6.02 \times 10^{23}$
$=1.4 \times 10^{22}$

</details>

### Q167. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the volume of unit cell having four particles in it with density $19.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ [molar mass of element $=190 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.32 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(B)** $5.0 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(C)** $6.64 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$
- **(D)** $2.4 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $6.64 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} d & =\frac{Z \times M}{V \times N_A} \\ 19 & =\frac{4 \times 190}{V \times 6.02 \times 10^{23}} \\ V & =\frac{40}{6.02} \times 10^{-23} \\ & =6.6 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q172. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

Edge length of unit cell of a crystal is $288 \mathrm{pm}$. If its density is $7.2 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ determine the type of unit cell (cat mass $=52$ ).

- **(B)** Simple cubic close
- **(C)** Face centered cubic
- **(D)** Body centered cubic

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

$\begin{aligned}
& 7.2=\frac{Z \times 52}{\left(2.88 \times 10^{-8}\right)^3 \times 6.02 \times 10^{23}} \\
& Z=1.991 \simeq 2.0
\end{aligned}$
Therefore, solid has BCC structure.

</details>

### Q179. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 2)

Calculate the number of unit cells in 3 grams metal that crystallises to simple cubic unit cell having edge length $336 \mathrm{pm}$. (density of metal $=9.4 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ )

- **(A)** $8.41 \times 10^{21}$

- **(D)** $5.82 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

$\begin{aligned} & 9.4=\frac{1 \times M}{\left(3.36 \times 10^{-8}\right)^3 \times 6.02 \times 10^{23}} \\ & M=214.6 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{mol}\end{aligned}$

- **(B)** $5.8 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$
- **(C)** $8.7 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$
- **(D)** $7.8 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $7.8 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{d}=\frac{\mathrm{Z} \times \mathrm{M}}{\mathrm{V} \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}} \\ & d=\frac{2 \times 56}{\left(2.88 \times 10^{-8}\right)^3 \times 6.02 \times 10^{23}} \\ & =7.78 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{cm}^3 \\ & \end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q186. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the number of atoms in 20 gram metal which crystalises to simple cubic structure having unit cell length $340 \mathrm{pm}$. (density of metal $=9.8 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.81 \times 10^{22}$
- **(B)** $5.19 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $5.42 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $4.95 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $5.19 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{d}=\frac{\mathrm{Z} \times \mathrm{M}}{\mathrm{V} \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}}$
$9.8=\frac{1 \times M}{\left(3.40 \times 10^{-8}\right)^3 \times 6.02 \times 10^{23}}$
$\begin{aligned} & M=\frac{9.8 \times 6.02 \times 39.3}{10} \\ & =231.87\end{aligned}$
Moles of atom in $20 \mathrm{~g}$ sample
$=\frac{20}{231.87}=0.08625$
No. of atoms $=0.08625 \times 6.02 \times 10^{23}$
$=5.19 \times 10^{22}$

</details>

### Q187. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The density of chromium metal is $7 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$. If edge length of unit cell is $300 \mathrm{pm}$, identify the type of unit cell. (At. mass $\mathrm{Cr}=52$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** Body centred cubic
- **(B)** Simple cubic
- **(C)** Hexagonal close pack
- **(D)** Face centred cubic

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Body centred cubic**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{d}=\frac{\mathrm{ZM}}{\mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}} \mathrm{a}^3} \\
& \mathrm{Z}=\frac{\mathrm{d} \cdot \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}} \cdot \mathrm{a}^3}{\mathrm{M}} \\
& \mathrm{a}=300 \mathrm{pm} \\
& \mathrm{a}=300 \times 10^{-10} \mathrm{~cm} \\
& \mathrm{a}=3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm} \\
& =\frac{7 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \times\left(3 \times 10^{-8}\right)^3}{52}
\end{aligned}$
Unit cell is Body centred cubic

</details>

### Q188. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

- **(B)** $150 \mathrm{pm}$
- **(C)** $300 \mathrm{pm}$
- **(D)** $\frac{300}{\sqrt{2}} \mathrm{pm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt{3} \times 150 \mathrm{pm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For BCC structure.
$\begin{aligned}
& \sqrt{3} \mathrm{a}=4 \mathrm{r} \\
& \mathrm{r}=\frac{\sqrt{3} \mathrm{a}}{4}=\frac{\sqrt{3} \times 600}{4} \\
& \mathrm{r}=\sqrt{3} \times 150 \mathrm{pm}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

$$

### Q190. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 1)

- **(B)** $\left(3.91 \times 10^{-20}\right)^{1 / 3} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $\left(3.01 \times 10^{-224}\right)^{1 / 3} \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $\left(3.30 \times 10^{-20}\right)^{1 / 3} \mathrm{~cm}$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

### Q193. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

How many particles per unit cell are present in BCC structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 3
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

In BCC structure atoms are present at each corner and at body centre of unit cell.
$$
\text { No. of atoms per unit cell }(Z)=\underbrace{8 \times \frac{1}{8}}_{\text {coner atoms }}+\underbrace{1 \times 1}_{\text {centreatoms }}
$$
$$
=2
$$

</details>

### Q196. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the volume occupied by particles in BCC structure if ' $a$ ' is edge length of unit cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{8}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi \mathrm{a}^3}{3 \sqrt{2}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi \mathrm{a}^3}{12 \sqrt{2}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{16}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{8}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of particles in BCC unit cell $(Z)=2$
Relation between edge length and radius,
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \sqrt{3} \mathrm{a}=4 \mathrm{r} \\
& \mathrm{r}=\frac{\sqrt{3} \mathrm{a}}{4}
\end{aligned}
$$
Volume occupied by particles in BCC $=2 \times$ Volume of one atom
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =2 \times \frac{4}{3} \pi \mathrm{r}^3 \\
& =2 \times \frac{4}{3} \pi\left(\frac{\sqrt{3} \mathrm{a}}{4}\right)^3 \\
& =\frac{2 \times 4}{3} \times \pi \times \frac{3 \sqrt{3} \mathrm{a}^3}{64} \\
& =\frac{\sqrt{3} \pi \mathrm{a}^3}{8}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q197. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A metal has BCC structure with edge length of unit cell $400 \mathrm{pm}$. Density of metal is $4 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ What is molar mass of metal?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $40 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $27 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $92 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $77 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $77 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{d}=\frac{\mathrm{ZM}}{\mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}} \cdot \mathrm{a}^3} \\ & \mathrm{a}=400 \mathrm{pm} \\ & =400 \times 10^{-10} \mathrm{~cm} \\ & =4 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~cm} \\ & \mathrm{Z}=2 \text { for BCC } \\ & 4=\frac{2 \times \mathrm{M}}{6.022 \times 10^{23} \times\left(4 \times 10^{-8}\right)^3} \\ & \mathrm{M}=\frac{4 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 64 \times 10^{-24}}{2} \\ & =77 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q198. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

How many atoms of niobium are present in $2.43 \mathrm{~g}$ if it forms bcc structure with density $9 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}$ and volume of unit cell $2.7 \times 10^{-23} \mathrm{~cm}^3 ?$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.01 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $4.1 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $5.0 \times 10^{22}$
- **(D)** $2.0 \times 10^{22}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2.0 \times 10^{22}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let number of atoms are $\mathrm{N}$
Mass of one atom $=2.43 / \mathrm{N}$
$\mathrm{d}=\frac{\mathrm{Z} \times \text { mass of one atom }}{\text { Volume of unit cell }}[\mathrm{Z}=2$ for $\mathrm{BCC}]$
$$
9=\frac{2 \times 2.43 / \mathrm{N}}{2.7 \times 10^{-23}} \quad \mathrm{~N}=\frac{2 \times 2.43}{2.7 \times 10^{-23} \times 9}=2 \times 10^{22}
$$

</details>

### Q200. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

How many tetrahedral voids are present in 0.4 mole of a compound that forms hcp structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.8 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $3.011 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $1.2 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $2.4 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $4.8 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In HCP structure for each atom one octahedral void and two tetrahedral voids will be present.
No. of atoms $=0.4 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=0.4 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}$
No. of tetrahedral voids $=$ No. of atoms $\times 2$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =0.4 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 2 \\
& =4.8 \times 10^{23}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q201. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

How many lattice points are present in a face centred cubic unit cell?

- **(A)** 8
- **(B)** 17
- **(C)** 14
- **(D)** 9

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 14**

**Detailed Solution:**

In face centred cubic unit cell atoms are present at each corner and at each face centre.
No.of lattice points $=$ no. of corners + no. of face centres
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =8+6 \\
& =14
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q202. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

What is the number of octahedral and tetrahedral voids presents respectively in 0.25 mole of a substance having hcp structure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.011 \times 10^{23}, 1.50 \times 10^{23}$
- **(B)** $6.011 \times 10^{23}, 3.011 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $3.011 \times 10^{23}, 6.022 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $1.50 \times 10^{23}, 3.011 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.50 \times 10^{23}, 3.011 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In HCP structure for each atom one octahedral void and two tetrahedral voids will be present.
No. Of atoms $=0.25 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=0.25 \times 6.022 \times 10^{23}$
No. of octahedral voids $=0.25 \mathrm{~N}_{\mathrm{A}}$ $=1.5 \times 10^{23}$
No. of tetrahedral voids $=2 \times 0.25 \times \mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}$ $=3.011 \times 10^{23}$

</details>
